1→"""Python extraction: function bodies, class structure, param patterns."""
     2→
     3→import hashlib
     4→import re
     5→from pathlib import Path
     6→
     7→from desloppify.engine.detectors.base import FunctionInfo
     8→from desloppify.engine.detectors.passthrough import (
     9→    classify_params,
    10→    classify_passthrough_tier,
    11→)
    12→from desloppify.core.discovery_api import find_py_files
    13→from desloppify.languages.python.extractors_classes import extract_py_classes
    14→from desloppify.languages.python.extractors_shared import (
    15→    extract_py_params,
    16→    find_block_end,
    17→    read_file,
    18→)
    19→
    20→
    21→def _find_signature_end(lines: list[str], start: int) -> int | None:
    22→    """Find the line where a function signature closes."""
    23→    for j in range(start, len(lines)):
    24→        lt = lines[j]
    25→        if ")" in lt and ":" in lt[lt.rindex(")") + 1 :]:
    26→            return j
    27→        if j > start and lt.strip().endswith(":"):
    28→            return j
    29→    return None
    30→
    31→
    32→def _extract_py_return_annotation(sig_text: str) -> str | None:
    33→    """Extract normalized return annotation from a Python signature."""
    34→    m = re.search(r"\)\s*->\s*(.*?)\s*:", sig_text, re.DOTALL)
    35→    if not m:
    36→        return None
    37→    annotation = " ".join(m.group(1).split())
    38→    return annotation or None
    39→
    40→
    41→def extract_py_functions(filepath: str) -> list[FunctionInfo]:
    42→    """Extract function bodies from a Python file using indentation-based boundaries."""
    43→    content = read_file(filepath)
    44→    if content is None:
    45→        return []
    46→    lines = content.splitlines()
    47→    functions = []
    48→    fn_re = re.compile(r"^(\s*)(?:async\s+)?def\s+(\w+)\s*\(")
    49→    i = 0
    50→    while i < len(lines):
    51→        m = fn_re.match(lines[i])
    52→        if not m:
    53→            i += 1
    54→            continue
    55→        fn_indent = len(m.group(1))
    56→        name = m.group(2)
    57→        start_line = i
    58→        j = _find_signature_end(lines, i)
    59→        if j is None:
    60→            i += 1
    61→            continue
    62→
    63→        # Extract params from multi-line signature
    64→        sig_text = "\n".join(lines[start_line : j + 1])
    65→        open_paren = sig_text.index("(")
    66→        if ")" not in sig_text:
    67→            i += 1
    68→            continue
    69→        close_paren = sig_text.rindex(")")
    70→        param_str = sig_text[open_paren + 1 : close_paren]
    71→        params = extract_py_params(param_str)
    72→
    73→        # Find body extent: all lines indented past fn_indent, trim trailing blanks
    74→        block_end = find_block_end(lines, j + 1, fn_indent)
    75→        end_line = block_end
    76→        while end_line > j + 1 and not lines[end_line - 1].strip():
    77→            end_line -= 1
    78→        body = "\n".join(lines[start_line:end_line])
    79→        normalized = normalize_py_body(body)
    80→        if len(normalized.splitlines()) >= 3:
    81→            functions.append(
    82→                FunctionInfo(
    83→                    name=name,
    84→                    file=filepath,
    85→                    line=start_line + 1,
    86→                    end_line=end_line,
    87→                    loc=end_line - start_line,
    88→                    body=body,
    89→                    normalized=normalized,
    90→                    body_hash=hashlib.md5(normalized.encode()).hexdigest(),
    91→                    params=params,
    92→                )
    93→            )
    94→        i = end_line
    95→    return functions
    96→
    97→
    98→def normalize_py_body(body: str) -> str:
    99→    """Normalize a Python function body: strip docstrings, comments, print/logging."""
   100→    lines = body.splitlines()
   101→    normalized = []
   102→    in_docstring = False
   103→    docstring_quote = None
   104→    for line in lines:
   105→        stripped = line.strip()
   106→        if in_docstring:
   107→            if docstring_quote and docstring_quote in stripped:
   108→                in_docstring = False
   109→            continue
   110→        if stripped.startswith('"""') or stripped.startswith("'''"):
   111→            docstring_quote = stripped[:3]
   112→            if stripped.count(docstring_quote) >= 2:
   113→                continue
   114→            in_docstring = True
   115→            continue
   116→        if not stripped or stripped.startswith("#"):
   117→            continue
   118→        cp = stripped.find("  #")
   119→        if cp > 0:
   120→            stripped = stripped[:cp].rstrip()
   121→        if re.match(r"(?:print\s*\(|(?:logging|logger|log)\.\w+\s*\()", stripped):
   122→            continue
   123→        if stripped:
   124→            normalized.append(stripped)
   125→    return "\n".join(normalized)
   126→
   127→
   128→def py_passthrough_pattern(name: str) -> str:
   129→    """Match same-name keyword arg: param=param in a function call."""
   130→    escaped = re.escape(name)
   131→    return rf"\b{escaped}\s*=\s*{escaped}\b"
   132→
   133→
   134→_PY_DEF_RE = re.compile(r"^def\s+(\w+)\s*\(", re.MULTILINE)
   135→
   136→
   137→def detect_passthrough_functions(path: Path) -> list[dict]:
   138→    """Detect Python functions where most params are same-name forwarded."""
   139→    entries = []
   140→    for filepath in find_py_files(path):
   141→        content = read_file(filepath)
   142→        if content is None:
   143→            continue
   144→        for m in _PY_DEF_RE.finditer(content):
   145→            name = m.group(1)
   146→            depth = 1
   147→            i = m.end()
   148→            while i < len(content) and depth > 0:
   149→                ch = content[i]
   150→                if ch == "(":
   151→                    depth += 1
   152→                elif ch == ")":
   153→                    depth -= 1
   154→                i += 1
   155→            if depth != 0:
   156→                continue
   157→            param_str = content[m.end() : i - 1]
   158→            params = extract_py_params(param_str)
   159→            if len(params) < 4:
   160→                continue
   161→            rest_after_paren = content[i:]
   162→            colon_m = re.search(r":", rest_after_paren)
   163→            if not colon_m:
   164→                continue
   165→            rest = rest_after_paren[colon_m.end() :]
   166→            bm = re.search(r"\n(?=[^\s\n#])", rest)
   167→            body = rest[: bm.start()] if bm else rest
   168→
   169→            has_kwargs_spread = bool(re.search(r"\*\*kwargs\b", body))
   170→            pt, direct = classify_params(
   171→                params, body, py_passthrough_pattern, occurrences_per_match=2
   172→            )
   173→
   174→            if len(pt) < 4 and not has_kwargs_spread:
   175→                continue
   176→            ratio = len(pt) / len(params)
   177→            classification = classify_passthrough_tier(
   178→                len(pt), ratio, has_spread=has_kwargs_spread
   179→            )
   180→            if classification is None:
   181→                continue
   182→            tier, confidence = classification
   183→
   184→            entries.append(
   185→                {
   186→                    "file": filepath,
   187→                    "function": name,
   188→                    "total_params": len(params),
   189→                    "passthrough": len(pt),
   190→                    "direct": len(direct),
   191→                    "ratio": round(ratio, 2),
   192→                    "line": content[: m.start()].count("\n") + 1,
   193→                    "tier": tier,
   194→                    "confidence": confidence,
   195→                    "passthrough_params": sorted(pt),
   196→                    "direct_params": sorted(direct),
   197→                    "has_kwargs_spread": has_kwargs_spread,
   198→                }
   199→            )
   200→
   201→    return sorted(entries, key=lambda e: (-e["passthrough"], -e["ratio"]))
   202→
   203→
   204→__all__ = [
   205→    "detect_passthrough_functions",
   206→    "extract_py_classes",
   207→    "extract_py_functions",
   208→    "extract_py_params",
   209→    "normalize_py_body",
   210→    "py_passthrough_pattern",
   211→]
   212→
```

1→"""Batch execution flow helpers for review command."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import json
     6→import logging
     7→import math
     8→import shlex
     9→import sys
    10→from dataclasses import dataclass
    11→from datetime import UTC, datetime
    12→from pathlib import Path
    13→from typing import Any
    14→from collections.abc import Callable
    15→
    16→from desloppify.core._internal.coercions import (
    17→    coerce_non_negative_float,
    18→    coerce_positive_float,
    19→    coerce_positive_int,
    20→)
    21→from desloppify.core.fallbacks import log_best_effort_failure, warn_best_effort
    22→from desloppify.intelligence.review.feedback_contract import (
    23→    LEGACY_REVIEW_QUALITY_HIGH_SCORE_MISSING_ISSUES_KEY,
    24→    REVIEW_QUALITY_HIGH_SCORE_MISSING_ISSUES_KEY,
    25→)
    26→from desloppify.intelligence.review.dimensions.data import load_dimensions_for_lang
    27→
    28→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    29→_RUN_LOG_WARNED_PATHS: set[Path] = set()
    30→
    31→
    32→def _append_run_log(run_log_path: Path, message: str) -> None:
    33→    """Append a timestamped message to the run log file."""
    34→    line = f"{datetime.now(UTC).isoformat(timespec='seconds')} {message}\n"
    35→    try:
    36→        with run_log_path.open("a", encoding="utf-8") as handle:
    37→            handle.write(line)
    38→    except OSError as exc:
    39→        log_best_effort_failure(logger, f"append review run log {run_log_path}", exc)
    40→        if run_log_path not in _RUN_LOG_WARNED_PATHS:
    41→            _RUN_LOG_WARNED_PATHS.add(run_log_path)
    42→            warn_best_effort(
    43→                f"Could not append review run log at {run_log_path}: {exc}"
    44→            )
    45→        return
    46→
    47→
    48→@dataclass
    49→class _ProgressCtx:
    50→    """Shared mutable state for the batch progress reporter."""
    51→
    52→    run_log_path: Path
    53→    colorize_fn: Callable[[str, str], str]
    54→    total_batches: int
    55→    batch_positions: dict[int, int]
    56→    batch_status: dict[str, dict[str, object]]
    57→    stall_warning_seconds: int
    58→    stall_warned_batches: set[int]
    59→
    60→
    61→def _report_progress(
    62→    ctx: _ProgressCtx,
    63→    batch_index: int | object,
    64→    event: str | None = None,
    65→    code: int | None = None,
    66→    **details,
    67→) -> None:
    68→    """Handle batch progress events (heartbeat, queued, start, done)."""
    69→    if event is None and hasattr(batch_index, "event"):
    70→        progress_event = batch_index
    71→        event = str(getattr(progress_event, "event", "")).strip()
    72→        code = getattr(progress_event, "code", code)
    73→        details_obj = getattr(progress_event, "details", {})
    74→        details = details_obj if isinstance(details_obj, dict) else {}
    75→        batch_index = int(getattr(progress_event, "batch_index", -1))
    76→    else:
    77→        batch_index = int(batch_index) if isinstance(batch_index, int | float) else -1
    78→
    79→    if not event:
    80→        return
    81→
    82→    if event == "heartbeat":
    83→        _handle_heartbeat_event(ctx, details)
    84→        return
    85→
    86→    position = ctx.batch_positions.get(batch_index, 0)
    87→    key = str(batch_index + 1)
    88→    state = ctx.batch_status.setdefault(
    89→        key, {"position": position, "status": "pending"},
    90→    )
    91→    if event == "queued":
    92→        state["status"] = "queued"
    93→        print(ctx.colorize_fn(f"  Batch {position}/{ctx.total_batches} queued (#{batch_index + 1})", "dim"))
    94→        _append_run_log(ctx.run_log_path, f"batch-queued batch={batch_index + 1} position={position}/{ctx.total_batches}")
    95→        return
    96→    if event == "start":
    97→        state["status"] = "running"
    98→        state["started_at"] = datetime.now(UTC).isoformat(timespec="seconds")
    99→        print(ctx.colorize_fn(f"  Batch {position}/{ctx.total_batches} started (#{batch_index + 1})", "dim"))
   100→        _append_run_log(ctx.run_log_path, f"batch-start batch={batch_index + 1} position={position}/{ctx.total_batches}")
   101→        return
   102→    if event == "done":
   103→        _handle_done_event(ctx, batch_index, position, state, code, details)
   104→
   105→
   106→def _handle_heartbeat_event(ctx: _ProgressCtx, details: dict) -> None:
   107→    """Process a heartbeat progress event."""
   108→    active = details.get("active_batches")
   109→    queued = details.get("queued_batches", [])
   110→    elapsed = details.get("elapsed_seconds", {})
   111→    if not isinstance(active, list):
   112→        active = []
   113→    if not isinstance(queued, list):
   114→        queued = []
   115→    if not active and not queued:
   116→        return
   117→    segments: list[str] = []
   118→    for idx in active[:6]:
   119→        secs = 0
   120→        if isinstance(elapsed, dict):
   121→            raw_secs = elapsed.get(idx, 0)
   122→            secs = int(raw_secs) if isinstance(raw_secs, int | float) else 0
   123→        segments.append(f"#{idx + 1}:{secs}s")
   124→    if len(active) > 6:
   125→        segments.append(f"+{len(active) - 6} more")
   126→    queued_segment = f", queued {len(queued)}" if queued else ""
   127→    print(
   128→        ctx.colorize_fn(
   129→            f"  Batch heartbeat: {len(active)}/{ctx.total_batches} active{queued_segment} "
   130→            f"({', '.join(segments) if segments else 'running batches pending'})",
   131→            "dim",
   132→        )
   133→    )
   134→    _append_run_log(
   135→        ctx.run_log_path,
   136→        "heartbeat "
   137→        f"active={[idx + 1 for idx in active]} queued={[idx + 1 for idx in queued]} "
   138→        f"elapsed={{{', '.join(f'{idx + 1}:{elapsed.get(idx, 0)}' for idx in active)}}}",
   139→    )
   140→    if ctx.stall_warning_seconds > 0 and isinstance(elapsed, dict):
   141→        slow_active = [
   142→            idx for idx in active
   143→            if isinstance(elapsed.get(idx), int | float)
   144→            and int(elapsed.get(idx) or 0) >= ctx.stall_warning_seconds
   145→        ]
   146→        newly_warned = [idx for idx in slow_active if idx not in ctx.stall_warned_batches]
   147→        if newly_warned:
   148→            ctx.stall_warned_batches.update(newly_warned)
   149→            print(
   150→                ctx.colorize_fn(
   151→                    f"  Stall warning: batches {[idx + 1 for idx in sorted(newly_warned)]} exceeded "
   152→                    f"{ctx.stall_warning_seconds}s elapsed. "
   153→                    "This may be normal for long runs; review run.log and batch logs.",
   154→                    "yellow",
   155→                )
   156→            )
   157→            _append_run_log(
   158→                ctx.run_log_path,
   159→                f"stall-warning threshold={ctx.stall_warning_seconds}s "
   160→                f"batches={[idx + 1 for idx in sorted(newly_warned)]}",
   161→            )
   162→
   163→
   164→def _handle_done_event(
   165→    ctx: _ProgressCtx,
   166→    batch_index: int,
   167→    position: int,
   168→    state: dict[str, object],
   169→    code: int | None,
   170→    details: dict,
   171→) -> None:
   172→    """Process a batch-done progress event."""
   173→    status = "done" if code == 0 else f"failed ({code})"
   174→    tone = "dim" if code == 0 else "yellow"
   175→    elapsed_seconds = details.get("elapsed_seconds")
   176→    elapsed_suffix = ""
   177→    if isinstance(elapsed_seconds, int | float):
   178→        elapsed_suffix = f" in {int(max(0, elapsed_seconds))}s"
   179→        state["elapsed_seconds"] = int(max(0, elapsed_seconds))
   180→    state["status"] = "succeeded" if code == 0 else "failed"
   181→    state["exit_code"] = int(code) if isinstance(code, int) else code
   182→    state["completed_at"] = datetime.now(UTC).isoformat(timespec="seconds")
   183→    if batch_index in ctx.stall_warned_batches:
   184→        ctx.stall_warned_batches.discard(batch_index)
   185→    print(
   186→        ctx.colorize_fn(
   187→            f"  Batch {position}/{ctx.total_batches} {status}{elapsed_suffix} (#{batch_index + 1})",
   188→            tone,
   189→        )
   190→    )
   191→    _append_run_log(
   192→        ctx.run_log_path,
   193→        f"batch-done batch={batch_index + 1} position={position}/{ctx.total_batches} "
   194→        f"code={code} elapsed={state.get('elapsed_seconds', 0)}",
   195→    )
   196→
   197→
   198→def _validate_runner(runner: str, *, colorize_fn) -> None:
   199→    """Validate review batch runner."""
   200→    if runner == "codex":
   201→        return
   202→    print(
   203→        colorize_fn(
   204→            f"  Error: unsupported runner '{runner}' (supported: codex)", "red"
   205→        ),
   206→        file=sys.stderr,
   207→    )
   208→    sys.exit(2)
   209→
   210→
   211→def _require_batches(
   212→    packet: dict,
   213→    *,
   214→    colorize_fn,
   215→    suggested_prepare_cmd: str | None = None,
   216→) -> list[dict]:
   217→    """Return investigation batches or exit with a clear error."""
   218→    batches = packet.get("investigation_batches", [])
   219→    if isinstance(batches, list) and batches:
   220→        return batches
   221→    print(
   222→        colorize_fn("  Error: packet has no investigation_batches.", "red"),
   223→        file=sys.stderr,
   224→    )
   225→    if isinstance(suggested_prepare_cmd, str) and suggested_prepare_cmd.strip():
   226→        print(
   227→            colorize_fn(
   228→                f"  Regenerate review context first: `{suggested_prepare_cmd}`",
   229→                "yellow",
   230→            ),
   231→            file=sys.stderr,
   232→        )
   233→    print(
   234→        colorize_fn(
   235→            "  Happy path: `desloppify review --run-batches --runner codex --parallel --scan-after-import`.",
   236→            "dim",
   237→        ),
   238→        file=sys.stderr,
   239→    )
   240→    sys.exit(1)

   243→def _print_review_quality(quality: object, *, colorize_fn) -> None:
   244→    """Render merged review quality summary when present."""
   245→    if not isinstance(quality, dict):
   246→        return
   247→    coverage = quality.get("dimension_coverage")
   248→    density = quality.get("evidence_density")
   249→    high_missing_issue_note = quality.get(
   250→        REVIEW_QUALITY_HIGH_SCORE_MISSING_ISSUES_KEY
   251→    )
   252→    if not isinstance(high_missing_issue_note, int | float):
   253→        high_missing_issue_note = quality.get(
   254→            LEGACY_REVIEW_QUALITY_HIGH_SCORE_MISSING_ISSUES_KEY
   255→        )
   256→    finding_pressure = quality.get("finding_pressure")
   257→    dims_with_findings = quality.get("dimensions_with_findings")
   258→    if not isinstance(coverage, int | float) or not isinstance(density, int | float):
   259→        return
   260→
   261→    pressure_segment = ""
   262→    if isinstance(finding_pressure, int | float) and isinstance(dims_with_findings, int):
   263→        pressure_segment = (
   264→            f", finding-pressure {float(finding_pressure):.2f} "
   265→            f"across {dims_with_findings} dims"
   266→        )
   267→    print(
   268→        colorize_fn(
   269→            "  Review quality: "
   270→            f"dimension coverage {float(coverage):.2f}, "
   271→            f"evidence density {float(density):.2f}, "
   272→            f"high-score-missing-issue-note {int(high_missing_issue_note or 0)}"
   273→            f"{pressure_segment}",
   274→            "dim",
   275→        )
   276→    )
   277→
   278→
   279→def _collect_reviewed_files_from_batches(
   280→    *,
   281→    batches: list[dict[str, object]],
   282→    selected_indexes: list[int],
   283→) -> list[str]:
   284→    """Collect normalized file paths reviewed in the selected batch set."""
   285→    reviewed: list[str] = []
   286→    seen: set[str] = set()
   287→    for idx in selected_indexes:
   288→        if idx < 0 or idx >= len(batches):
   289→            continue
   290→        batch = batches[idx]
   291→        files = batch.get("files_to_read", [])
   292→        if not isinstance(files, list):
   293→            continue
   294→        for raw in files:
   295→            if not isinstance(raw, str):
   296→                continue
   297→            path = raw.strip().strip(",'\"")
   298→            if not path or path in {".", ".."}:
   299→                continue
   300→            if path.endswith("/"):
   301→                continue
   302→            if path in seen:
   303→                continue
   304→            seen.add(path)
   305→            reviewed.append(path)
   306→    return reviewed
   307→
   308→
   309→def _normalize_dimension_list(raw: object) -> list[str]:
   310→    """Normalize dimension collections to a stable, de-duplicated list."""
   311→    if not isinstance(raw, list):
   312→        return []
   313→    out: list[str] = []
   314→    seen: set[str] = set()
   315→    for item in raw:
   316→        if not isinstance(item, str):
   317→            continue
   318→        dim = item.strip()
   319→        if not dim or dim in seen:
   320→            continue
   321→        seen.add(dim)
   322→        out.append(dim)
   323→    return out
   324→
   325→
   326→def _scored_dimensions_for_lang(lang_name: str) -> list[str]:
   327→    """Return default scored subjective dimensions for one language."""
   328→    try:
   329→        default_dims, _, _ = load_dimensions_for_lang(lang_name)
   330→    except (ValueError, RuntimeError):
   331→        return []
   332→    return _normalize_dimension_list(default_dims)
   333→
   334→
   335→def _missing_scored_dimensions(
   336→    *,
   337→    selected_dims: list[str],
   338→    scored_dims: list[str],
   339→) -> list[str]:
   340→    selected = set(selected_dims)
   341→    return [dim for dim in scored_dims if dim not in selected]
   342→
   343→
   344→def _missing_dimensions_command(*, missing_dims: list[str], scan_path: str) -> str:
   345→    """Return rerun command for missing subjective dimensions."""
   346→    base = "desloppify review --run-batches --runner codex --parallel --scan-after-import"
   347→    if scan_path and scan_path != ".":
   348→        base += f" --path {shlex.quote(scan_path)}"
   349→    if missing_dims:
   350→        base += f" --dimensions {','.join(missing_dims)}"
   351→    return base
   352→
   353→
   354→def _print_preflight_dimension_scope_notice(
   355→    *,
   356→    selected_dims: list[str],
   357→    scored_dims: list[str],
   358→    explicit_selection: bool,
   359→    scan_path: str,
   360→    colorize_fn,
   361→) -> None:
   362→    """Print trigger-time notice when run scope is a scored-dimension subset."""
   363→    if not scored_dims:
   364→        return
   365→    missing_dims = _missing_scored_dimensions(
   366→        selected_dims=selected_dims,
   367→        scored_dims=scored_dims,
   368→    )
   369→    if not missing_dims:
   370→        return
   371→
   372→    covered_count = len([dim for dim in selected_dims if dim in set(scored_dims)])
   373→    scope_reason = (
   374→        "explicit --dimensions selection"
   375→        if explicit_selection
   376→        else "language default review dimension set"
   377→    )
   378→    tone = "yellow" if explicit_selection else "red"
   379→    print(
   380→        colorize_fn(
   381→            "  WARNING: this run targets "
   382→            f"{covered_count}/{len(scored_dims)} scored subjective dimensions "
   383→            f"({scope_reason}).",
   384→            tone,
   385→        )
   386→    )
   387→    preview = ", ".join(missing_dims[:5])
   388→    if len(missing_dims) > 5:
   389→        preview = f"{preview}, +{len(missing_dims) - 5} more"
   390→    print(colorize_fn(f"  Missing from this run: {preview}", "yellow"))
   391→    print(
   392→        colorize_fn(
   393→            "  Rerun missing dimensions: "
   394→            f"`{_missing_dimensions_command(missing_dims=missing_dims, scan_path=scan_path)}`",
   395→            "dim",
   396→        )
   397→    )
   398→
   399→
   400→def _print_import_dimension_coverage_notice(
   401→    *,
   402→    assessed_dims: list[str],
   403→    scored_dims: list[str],
   404→    scan_path: str,
   405→    colorize_fn,
   406→) -> list[str]:
   407→    """Print result-time notice when merged import covers only a subset."""
   408→    if not scored_dims:
   409→        return []
   410→    missing_dims = _missing_scored_dimensions(
   411→        selected_dims=assessed_dims,
   412→        scored_dims=scored_dims,
   413→    )
   414→    if not missing_dims:
   415→        return []
   416→
   417→    covered_count = len([dim for dim in assessed_dims if dim in set(scored_dims)])
   418→    print(
   419→        colorize_fn(
   420→            "  Coverage gap: imported assessments for "
   421→            f"{covered_count}/{len(scored_dims)} scored subjective dimensions.",
   422→            "yellow",
   423→        )
   424→    )
   425→    preview = ", ".join(missing_dims[:5])
   426→    if len(missing_dims) > 5:
   427→        preview = f"{preview}, +{len(missing_dims) - 5} more"
   428→    print(colorize_fn(f"  Still missing: {preview}", "yellow"))
   429→    print(
   430→        colorize_fn(
   431→            "  Run to cover missing dimensions: "
   432→            f"`{_missing_dimensions_command(missing_dims=missing_dims, scan_path=scan_path)}`",
   433→            "dim",
   434→        )
   435→    )
   436→    return missing_dims
   437→
   438→
   439→def do_run_batches(
   440→    args,
   441→    state,
   442→    lang,
   443→    state_file,
   444→    *,
   445→    config: dict | None,
   446→    run_stamp_fn,
   447→    load_or_prepare_packet_fn,
   448→    selected_batch_indexes_fn,
   449→    prepare_run_artifacts_fn,
   450→    run_codex_batch_fn,
   451→    execute_batches_fn,
   452→    collect_batch_results_fn,
   453→    print_failures_fn,
   454→    print_failures_and_exit_fn,
   455→    merge_batch_results_fn,
   456→    build_import_provenance_fn,
   457→    do_import_fn,
   458→    run_followup_scan_fn,
   459→    safe_write_text_fn,
   460→    colorize_fn,
   461→    project_root: Path,
   462→    subagent_runs_dir: Path,
   463→) -> None:
   464→    """Run holistic investigation batches with a local subagent runner."""
   465→    config = config or {}
   466→    runner = getattr(args, "runner", "codex")
   467→    _validate_runner(runner, colorize_fn=colorize_fn)
   468→    allow_partial = bool(getattr(args, "allow_partial", False))
   469→    run_parallel = bool(getattr(args, "parallel", False))
   470→    max_parallel_batches = coerce_positive_int(
   471→        getattr(args, "max_parallel_batches", None),
   472→        default=3,
   473→        minimum=1,
   474→    )
   475→    heartbeat_seconds = coerce_positive_float(
   476→        getattr(args, "batch_heartbeat_seconds", None),
   477→        default=15.0,
   478→        minimum=0.1,
   479→    )
   480→    batch_timeout_seconds = coerce_positive_int(
   481→        getattr(args, "batch_timeout_seconds", None),
   482→        default=2 * 60 * 60,
   483→        minimum=1,
   484→    )
   485→    batch_max_retries = coerce_positive_int(
   486→        getattr(args, "batch_max_retries", None),
   487→        default=1,
   488→        minimum=0,
   489→    )
   490→    batch_retry_backoff_seconds = coerce_non_negative_float(
   491→        getattr(args, "batch_retry_backoff_seconds", None),
   492→        default=2.0,
   493→    )
   494→    stall_warning_seconds = coerce_positive_int(
   495→        getattr(args, "batch_stall_warning_seconds", None),
   496→        default=0,
   497→        minimum=0,
   498→    )
   499→    stall_kill_seconds = coerce_positive_int(
   500→        getattr(args, "batch_stall_kill_seconds", None),
   501→        default=0,
   502→        minimum=0,
   503→    )
   504→
   505→    stamp = run_stamp_fn()
   506→    packet, immutable_packet_path, prompt_packet_path = load_or_prepare_packet_fn(
   507→        args,
   508→        state=state,
   509→        lang=lang,
   510→        config=config,
   511→        stamp=stamp,
   512→    )
   513→
   514→    scan_path = str(getattr(args, "path", ".") or ".")
   515→    packet_dimensions = _normalize_dimension_list(packet.get("dimensions", []))
   516→    scored_dimensions = _scored_dimensions_for_lang(lang.name)
   517→    _print_preflight_dimension_scope_notice(
   518→        selected_dims=packet_dimensions,
   519→        scored_dims=scored_dimensions,
   520→        explicit_selection=bool(getattr(args, "dimensions", None)),
   521→        scan_path=scan_path,
   522→        colorize_fn=colorize_fn,
   523→    )
   524→    suggested_prepare_cmd = f"desloppify review --prepare --path {scan_path}"
   525→    batches = _require_batches(
   526→        packet,
   527→        colorize_fn=colorize_fn,
   528→        suggested_prepare_cmd=suggested_prepare_cmd,
   529→    )
   530→
   531→    selected_indexes = selected_batch_indexes_fn(args, batch_count=len(batches))
   532→    total_batches = len(selected_indexes)
   533→    effective_workers = min(total_batches, max_parallel_batches) if run_parallel else 1
   534→    waves = max(1, math.ceil(total_batches / max(1, effective_workers)))
   535→    worst_case_seconds = waves * batch_timeout_seconds
   536→    worst_case_minutes = max(1, math.ceil(worst_case_seconds / 60))
   537→    print(
   538→        colorize_fn(
   539→            "  Runtime expectation: "
   540→            f"{total_batches} batch(es), workers={effective_workers}, "
   541→            f"timeout-per-batch={int(batch_timeout_seconds / 60)}m, "
   542→            f"worst-case upper bound ~{worst_case_minutes}m.",
   543→            "dim",
   544→        )
   545→    )
   546→    run_dir, logs_dir, prompt_files, output_files, log_files = prepare_run_artifacts_fn(
   547→        stamp=stamp,
   548→        selected_indexes=selected_indexes,
   549→        batches=batches,
   550→        packet_path=prompt_packet_path,
   551→        run_root=subagent_runs_dir,
   552→        repo_root=project_root,
   553→    )
   554→    raw_run_log_file = getattr(args, "run_log_file", None)
   555→    run_log_file_value = (
   556→        raw_run_log_file.strip()
   557→        if isinstance(raw_run_log_file, str) and raw_run_log_file.strip()
   558→        else None
   559→    )
   560→    if run_log_file_value is not None:
   561→        candidate = Path(run_log_file_value).expanduser()
   562→        run_log_path = candidate if candidate.is_absolute() else project_root / candidate
   563→    else:
   564→        run_log_path = run_dir / "run.log"
   565→    run_log_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   566→    _append_run_log(run_log_path,
   567→        "run-start "

   571→        f"retries={batch_max_retries} "

   573→        f"selected={[idx + 1 for idx in selected_indexes]}"
   574→    )
   575→    _append_run_log(run_log_path, f"run-path {run_dir}")
   576→    _append_run_log(run_log_path, f"packet {immutable_packet_path}")
   577→    _append_run_log(run_log_path, f"blind-packet {prompt_packet_path}")

   581→        print(

   591→
   592→    batch_positions = {batch_idx: pos + 1 for pos, batch_idx in enumerate(selected_indexes)}

   623→    from functools import partial
   624→    bound_progress = partial(_report_progress, progress_ctx)
   625→    run_summary_path = run_dir / "run_summary.json"
   626→
   627→    def _write_run_summary(
   628→        *,
   629→        successful_indexes: list[int],
   630→        failure_set: set[int],
   631→        interrupted: bool = False,
   632→        interruption_reason: str | None = None,
   633→    ) -> dict[str, object]:
   634→        payload: dict[str, object] = {
   635→            "created_at": summary_created_at,
   636→            "run_stamp": stamp,
   637→            "runner": runner,
   638→            "parallel": run_parallel,
   639→            "selected_batches": [idx + 1 for idx in selected_indexes],
   640→            "successful_batches": [idx + 1 for idx in successful_indexes],
   641→            "failed_batches": [idx + 1 for idx in sorted(failure_set)],
   642→            "allow_partial": allow_partial,
   643→            "max_parallel_batches": max_parallel_batches if run_parallel else 1,
   644→            "batch_timeout_seconds": batch_timeout_seconds,
   645→            "batch_max_retries": batch_max_retries,
   646→            "batch_retry_backoff_seconds": batch_retry_backoff_seconds,
   647→            "batch_heartbeat_seconds": heartbeat_seconds if run_parallel else None,
   648→            "batch_stall_warning_seconds": stall_warning_seconds if run_parallel else None,
   649→            "batch_stall_kill_seconds": stall_kill_seconds,
   650→            "immutable_packet": str(immutable_packet_path),
   651→            "blind_packet": str(prompt_packet_path),
   652→            "run_dir": str(run_dir),
   653→            "logs_dir": str(logs_dir),
   654→            "run_log": str(run_log_path),
   655→            "batches": progress_ctx.batch_status,
   656→        }
   657→        if interrupted:
   658→            payload["interrupted"] = True
   659→            if isinstance(interruption_reason, str) and interruption_reason.strip():
   660→                payload["interruption_reason"] = interruption_reason
   661→        safe_write_text_fn(run_summary_path, json.dumps(payload, indent=2) + "\n")
   662→        return payload
   663→
   664→    def _mark_inflight_batches_interrupted() -> None:
   665→        completed_at = datetime.now(UTC).isoformat(timespec="seconds")
   666→        for idx in selected_indexes:
   667→            key = str(idx + 1)
   668→            state = progress_ctx.batch_status.setdefault(
   669→                key,
   670→                {"position": batch_positions.get(idx, 0), "status": "pending"},
   671→            )
   672→            status = str(state.get("status", "pending"))
   673→            if status in {"pending", "queued", "running"}:
   674→                state["status"] = "interrupted"
   675→                state["completed_at"] = completed_at
   676→
   677→    # Persist a baseline summary early so interrupted runs still leave usable metadata.
   678→    _write_run_summary(successful_indexes=[], failure_set=set())
   679→
   680→    def _make_task(idx):
   681→        def task():
   682→            return run_codex_batch_fn(
   683→                repo_root=project_root,
   684→                prompt=prompt_files[idx].read_text(),
   685→                output_file=output_files[idx],
   686→                log_file=log_files[idx],
   687→            )
   688→        return task
   689→
   690→    def _write_runner_exception(idx: int, exc: Exception) -> None:
   691→        log_path = log_files.get(idx)
   692→        if isinstance(log_path, Path):
   693→            safe_write_text_fn(log_path, f"Runner exception:\n{exc}\n")
   694→            return
   695→        _append_run_log(run_log_path, f"runner-exception batch={idx} error={exc}")
   696→
   697→    try:
   698→        execution_failures = execute_batches_fn(
   699→            tasks={idx: _make_task(idx) for idx in selected_indexes},
   700→            run_parallel=run_parallel,
   701→            progress_fn=bound_progress,
   702→            error_log_fn=_write_runner_exception,
   703→            max_parallel_workers=max_parallel_batches,
   704→            heartbeat_seconds=heartbeat_seconds,
   705→        )
   706→    except KeyboardInterrupt:
   707→        _mark_inflight_batches_interrupted()
   708→        _write_run_summary(
   709→            successful_indexes=[],
   710→            failure_set=set(),
   711→            interrupted=True,
   712→            interruption_reason="keyboard_interrupt",
   713→        )
   714→        _append_run_log(
   715→            run_log_path,
   716→            f"run-interrupted reason=keyboard_interrupt summary={run_summary_path}",
   717→        )
   718→        print(
   719→            colorize_fn(
   720→                "  Run interrupted (keyboard interrupt). "
   721→                f"Partial status saved at {run_summary_path}",
   722→                "yellow",
   723→            ),
   724→            file=sys.stderr,
   725→        )
   726→        raise SystemExit(130) from None
   727→    except Exception as exc:
   728→        _mark_inflight_batches_interrupted()
   729→        _write_run_summary(
   730→            successful_indexes=[],
   731→            failure_set=set(),
   732→            interrupted=True,
   733→            interruption_reason=f"exception:{type(exc).__name__}",
   734→        )
   735→        _append_run_log(
   736→            run_log_path,
   737→            f"run-aborted error={type(exc).__name__}:{exc} summary={run_summary_path}",
   738→        )
   739→        raise
   740→
   741→    allowed_dims = {
   742→        str(dim) for dim in packet.get("dimensions", []) if isinstance(dim, str)
   743→    }
   744→    batch_results, failures = collect_batch_results_fn(
   745→        selected_indexes=selected_indexes,
   746→        failures=execution_failures,
   747→        output_files=output_files,
   748→        allowed_dims=allowed_dims,
   749→    )
   750→
   751→    execution_failure_set = set(execution_failures)
   752→    failure_set = set(failures)
   753→    successful_indexes = sorted(idx for idx in selected_indexes if idx not in failure_set)
   754→    batch_status = progress_ctx.batch_status
   755→    for idx in selected_indexes:
   756→        key = str(idx + 1)
   757→        state = batch_status.setdefault(
   758→            key,
   759→            {"position": batch_positions.get(idx, 0), "status": "pending"},
   760→        )
   761→        if idx not in failure_set:
   762→            state["status"] = "succeeded"
   763→            continue
   764→        if idx in execution_failure_set:
   765→            state["status"] = "failed"
   766→            continue
   767→        if not output_files[idx].exists():
   768→            state["status"] = "missing_output"
   769→            continue
   770→        state["status"] = "parse_failed"
   771→
   772→    _write_run_summary(
   773→        successful_indexes=successful_indexes,
   774→        failure_set=failure_set,
   775→    )
   776→    print(colorize_fn(f"  Run summary: {run_summary_path}", "dim"))

   801→            colorize_fn=colorize_fn,

   803→        _append_run_log(

   805→            "run-partial "
   806→            f"successful={[idx + 1 for idx in successful_indexes]} "
   807→            f"failed={[idx + 1 for idx in sorted(failure_set)]}",
   808→        )
   809→
   810→    merged = merge_batch_results_fn(batch_results)
   811→    reviewed_files = _collect_reviewed_files_from_batches(

   814→    )
   815→    full_sweep_included = any(

   817→        for idx in successful_indexes
   818→        if 0 <= idx < len(batches)

   820→        if isinstance(batch, dict)

   822→    review_scope: dict[str, object] = {

   824→        "successful_batch_count": len(successful_indexes),
   825→        "full_sweep_included": full_sweep_included,

   828→    if isinstance(total_files, int) and not isinstance(total_files, bool) and total_files > 0:

   830→    merged["review_scope"] = review_scope

   832→        merged["reviewed_files"] = reviewed_files

   855→    merged_imported_dims = _normalize_dimension_list(

   858→    review_scope["imported_dimensions"] = merged_imported_dims
   859→    missing_after_import = _print_import_dimension_coverage_notice(

   865→    merged["assessment_coverage"] = {

   868→        "imported_dimensions": merged_assessment_dims,
   869→        "missing_dimensions": missing_after_import,

   871→    merged_path = run_dir / "holistic_findings_merged.json"
   872→    safe_write_text_fn(merged_path, json.dumps(merged, indent=2) + "\n")
   873→    print(colorize_fn(f"\n  Merged outputs: {merged_path}", "bold"))
   874→    _print_review_quality(merged.get("review_quality", {}), colorize_fn=colorize_fn)

   876→    try:

1→"""Tests for desloppify.languages.python.extractors — function/class extraction."""
     2→
     3→import textwrap
     4→from pathlib import Path
     5→
     6→from desloppify.languages.python.extractors import (
     7→    detect_passthrough_functions,
     8→    extract_py_classes,

    14→# ── Helpers ────────────────────────────────────────────────

    16→
    17→def _write_py(tmp_path: Path, code: str, filename: str = "test_mod.py") -> str:
    18→    """Write a Python file and return its path as a string."""
    19→    f = tmp_path / filename
    20→    f.write_text(textwrap.dedent(code))
    21→    return str(f)
    22→
    23→
    24→# ── extract_py_functions ──────────────────────────────────
    25→
    26→
    27→class TestExtractPyFunctions:
    28→    def test_simple_function(self, tmp_path):
    29→        fp = _write_py(
    30→            tmp_path,
    31→            """\
    32→            def greet(name):
    33→                greeting = f"Hello, {name}"
    34→                return greeting
    35→        """,
    36→        )
    37→        funcs = extract_py_functions(fp)
    38→        assert len(funcs) >= 1
    39→        fn = funcs[0]
    40→        assert fn.name == "greet"
    41→        assert fn.line == 1
    42→        assert fn.loc >= 3
    43→
    44→    def test_async_function(self, tmp_path):
    45→        fp = _write_py(
    46→            tmp_path,
    47→            """\
    48→            async def fetch(url):
    49→                response = await get(url)
    50→                return response
    51→        """,
    52→        )
    53→        funcs = extract_py_functions(fp)
    54→        assert len(funcs) >= 1
    55→        assert funcs[0].name == "fetch"
    56→
    57→    def test_function_params_extracted(self, tmp_path):
    58→        fp = _write_py(

    61→            def process(input_data, output_path, verbose=False):
    62→                if verbose:
    63→                    print(input_data)
    64→                return output_path
    65→        """,
    66→        )
    67→        funcs = extract_py_functions(fp)
    68→        assert len(funcs) >= 1
    69→        assert "input_data" in funcs[0].params
    70→        assert "output_path" in funcs[0].params
    71→        assert "verbose" in funcs[0].params
    72→
    73→    def test_self_excluded_from_params(self, tmp_path):
    74→        fp = _write_py(

    77→            class Foo:

    82→        )
    83→        funcs = extract_py_functions(fp)
    84→        method = [f for f in funcs if f.name == "method"]
    85→        assert len(method) >= 1

    88→    def test_multiple_functions(self, tmp_path):

   197→def _do_run_batches(args, state, lang, state_file, config: dict | None = None) -> None:
   198→    """Run holistic investigation batches with a local subagent runner."""
   199→    runtime_project_root = _runtime_project_root()

   207→        default=1,
   208→        minimum=0,
   209→    )
   210→    batch_retry_backoff_seconds = coerce_non_negative_float(
   211→        getattr(args, "batch_retry_backoff_seconds", None),
   212→        default=2.0,
   213→    )
   214→    batch_heartbeat_seconds = coerce_non_negative_float(
   215→        getattr(args, "batch_heartbeat_seconds", None),
   216→        default=15.0,
   217→    )
   218→    batch_live_log_interval_seconds = (
   219→        max(1.0, min(batch_heartbeat_seconds, 10.0))
   220→        if batch_heartbeat_seconds > 0
   221→        else 5.0
   222→    )
   223→    batch_stall_kill_seconds = coerce_non_negative_int(
   224→        getattr(args, "batch_stall_kill_seconds", None),
   225→        default=CODEX_BATCH_STALL_KILL_SECONDS,
   226→    )
   227→
   228→    def _prepare_run_artifacts(*, stamp, selected_indexes, batches, packet_path, run_root, repo_root):

    43→def _apply_persisted_exclusions(args, config: dict):
    44→    """Merge CLI --exclude with persisted config.exclude and apply globally."""
    45→    cli_exclusions = getattr(args, "exclude", None) or []
    46→    persisted = config.get("exclude", [])
    47→    combined = list(cli_exclusions) + [e for e in persisted if e not in cli_exclusions]
    48→    if not combined:
    49→        return
    50→    set_exclusions(combined)
    51→    if cli_exclusions:
    52→        print(
    53→            colorize(f"  Excluding: {', '.join(combined)}", "dim"),
    54→            file=sys.stderr,

    65→def _resolve_default_path(args) -> None:
    66→    """Fill args.path from detected language or default source path.
    67→
    68→    For the review command, the last scan path (stored in state) is used as the
    69→    default so that ``desloppify review --prepare`` works on the same scope as

    76→        if PROJECT_ROOT is not _PROJECT_ROOT_SENTINEL and isinstance(PROJECT_ROOT, Path)

    78→    )

    90→    lang = resolve_lang(args)

    93→    else:

    99→    config = load_config()

   104→
   105→    args.runtime = CommandRuntime(config=config, state=state, state_path=state_file)

   114→def _handle_help_command(args, parser) -> None:
   115→    """Handle explicit help command when present in parser config."""
   116→    topic = list(getattr(args, "topic", []) or [])
   117→    try:
   118→        parser.parse_args([*topic, "--help"])
   119→    except SystemExit:
   120→        return
   121→
   122→
   123→def main() -> None:
   124→    # Ensure Unicode output works on Windows terminals (cp1252 etc.)
   125→    for stream in (sys.stdout, sys.stderr):
   126→        if hasattr(stream, "reconfigure"):
   127→            try:
   128→                stream.reconfigure(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")
   129→            except (AttributeError, OSError):
   130→                logger.debug(
   131→                    "Skipping stream reconfigure for %s (not supported)",
   132→                    getattr(stream, "name", "<stream>"),
   133→                )
   134→
   135→    parser = create_parser()
   136→    args = parser.parse_args()
   137→    if args.command == "help":
   138→        _handle_help_command(args, parser)
   139→        return
   140→
   141→    try:
   142→        with runtime_scope():
   143→            _resolve_default_path(args)
   144→            _load_shared_runtime(args)
   145→
   146→            handler = _resolve_handler(args.command)
   147→            handler(args)
   148→    except LangResolutionError as exc:
   149→        print(colorize(f"  {exc.message}", "red"), file=sys.stderr)
   150→        sys.exit(1)
   151→    except KeyboardInterrupt:
   152→        print("\nInterrupted.")
   153→        sys.exit(1)
   154→
   155→
   156→if __name__ == "__main__":
   157→    main()

1129→def collect_batch_results(
  1130→    *,
  1131→    selected_indexes: list[int],
  1132→    failures: list[int],
  1133→    output_files: dict[int, Path],
  1134→    allowed_dims: set[str],
  1135→    extract_payload_fn,
  1136→    normalize_result_fn,
  1137→) -> tuple[list[BatchResult], list[int]]:
  1138→    """Parse and normalize batch outputs, preserving prior failures."""
  1139→    batch_results: list[BatchResult] = []
  1140→    failure_set = set(failures)
  1141→    for idx in selected_indexes:
  1142→        had_execution_failure = idx in failure_set
  1143→        raw_path = output_files[idx]
  1144→        payload = None
  1145→        parsed_from_log = False
  1146→        if raw_path.exists():
  1147→            try:
  1148→                payload = extract_payload_fn(raw_path.read_text())
  1149→            except OSError:
  1150→                payload = None
  1151→        if payload is None:
  1152→            payload = _extract_payload_from_log(idx, raw_path, extract_payload_fn)
  1153→            parsed_from_log = payload is not None
  1154→        if payload is None:
  1155→            failure_set.add(idx)
  1156→            continue
  1157→        if parsed_from_log:
  1158→            try:
  1159→                raw_path.write_text(json.dumps(payload, indent=2) + "\n")
  1160→            except OSError:
  1161→                pass
  1162→        try:
  1163→            assessments, findings, dimension_notes, quality = normalize_result_fn(
  1164→                payload,
  1165→                allowed_dims,
  1166→            )
  1167→        except ValueError:
  1168→            failure_set.add(idx)
  1169→            continue
  1170→        if had_execution_failure:
  1171→            failure_set.discard(idx)
  1172→        batch_results.append(
  1173→            BatchResult(
  1174→                batch_index=idx + 1,
  1175→                assessments=assessments,
  1176→                dimension_notes=dimension_notes,
  1177→                findings=findings,
  1178→                quality=quality,
  1179→            )
  1180→        )
  1181→    return batch_results, sorted(failure_set)
  1182→
  1183→
  1184→def _classify_runner_failure(log_text: str) -> str:
  1185→    """Classify batch failure type from log contents."""
  1186→    text = log_text.lower()
  1187→    if "timeout after" in text:
  1188→        return "timeout"
  1189→    if any(phrase in text for phrase in _TRANSIENT_RUNNER_PHRASES):
  1190→        return "stream_disconnect"
  1191→    if (
  1192→        "codex not found" in text
  1193→        or ("no such file or directory" in text and "$ codex " in text)
  1194→        or ("errno 2" in text and "codex" in text)
  1195→    ):
  1196→        return "runner_missing"
  1197→    if any(
  1198→        phrase in text
  1199→        for phrase in (
  1200→            "not authenticated",
  1201→            "authentication failed",
  1202→            "unauthorized",
  1203→            "forbidden",
  1204→            "login required",
  1205→            "please login",
  1206→            "access token",
  1207→        )
  1208→    ):
  1209→        return "runner_auth"
  1210→    if "runner exception" in text:
  1211→        return "runner_exception"
  1212→    return "unknown"
  1213→
  1214→
  1215→def _has_codex_backend_connectivity_issue(log_text: str) -> bool:
  1216→    """Return True when logs indicate Codex backend URL is unreachable."""
  1217→    text = log_text.lower()
  1218→    if "error sending request for url" not in text:
  1219→        return False
  1220→    return (
  1221→        _CODEX_BACKEND_PATH_HINT in text
  1222→        or _CODEX_BACKEND_HOST_HINT in text
  1223→        or "nodename nor servname provided" in text
  1224→        or "name or service not known" in text
  1225→        or "temporary failure in name resolution" in text
  1226→    )
  1227→
  1228→
  1229→def _looks_like_restricted_sandbox(log_text: str) -> bool:
  1230→    """Return True when logs resemble a constrained agent sandbox execution."""
  1231→    text = log_text.lower()
  1232→    return any(phrase in text for phrase in _SANDBOX_PATH_WARNING_PHRASES)
  1233→
  1234→
  1235→def _summarize_failure_categories(*, failures: list[int], logs_dir: Path) -> dict[str, int]:
  1236→    """Return counts by failure category for failed batches."""
  1237→    categories: dict[str, int] = {}
  1238→    for idx in sorted(set(failures)):
  1239→        log_file = logs_dir / f"batch-{idx + 1}.log"
  1240→        if not log_file.exists():
  1241→            category = "missing_log"
  1242→        else:
  1243→            try:
  1244→                category = _classify_runner_failure(log_file.read_text())
  1245→            except OSError:
  1246→                category = "log_read_error"
  1247→        categories[category] = categories.get(category, 0) + 1
  1248→    return categories
  1249→
  1250→
  1251→def _runner_failure_hints(*, failures: list[int], logs_dir: Path) -> list[str]:
  1252→    """Infer common runner environment failures from batch logs."""
  1253→    hints: list[str] = []
  1254→    for idx in sorted(set(failures)):
  1255→        log_file = logs_dir / f"batch-{idx + 1}.log"
  1256→        try:
  1257→            raw = log_file.read_text()
  1258→        except OSError:
  1259→            continue
  1260→        text = raw.lower()
  1261→        if (
  1262→            "codex not found" in text

21→def _find_signature_end(lines: list[str], start: int) -> int | None:
    22→    """Find the line where a function signature closes."""
    23→    for j in range(start, len(lines)):
    24→        lt = lines[j]
    25→        if ")" in lt and ":" in lt[lt.rindex(")") + 1 :]:
    26→            return j
    27→        if j > start and lt.strip().endswith(":"):
    28→            return j
    29→    return None
    30→
    31→
    32→def _extract_py_return_annotation(sig_text: str) -> str | None:
    33→    """Extract normalized return annotation from a Python signature."""
    34→    m = re.search(r"\)\s*->\s*(.*?)\s*:", sig_text, re.DOTALL)
    35→    if not m:
    36→        return None
    37→    annotation = " ".join(m.group(1).split())
    38→    return annotation or None
    39→
    40→
    41→def extract_py_functions(filepath: str) -> list[FunctionInfo]:
    42→    """Extract function bodies from a Python file using indentation-based boundaries."""
    43→    content = read_file(filepath)
    44→    if content is None:
    45→        return []
    46→    lines = content.splitlines()
    47→    functions = []
    48→    fn_re = re.compile(r"^(\s*)(?:async\s+)?def\s+(\w+)\s*\(")

    50→    while i < len(lines):

    53→            i += 1
    54→            continue
    55→        fn_indent = len(m.group(1))

    67→            i += 1

    43→            registry_state.register(name, cfg)  # store instance
    44→        else:
    45→            registry_state.register(name, cls)  # test doubles
    46→        return cls
    47→
    48→    return decorator

1→"""Config instantiation and public language resolution helpers."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from pathlib import Path
     6→
     7→from . import registry_state
     8→from .base.types import LangConfig
     9→from .contract_validation import validate_lang_contract
    10→from .discovery import load_all
    11→
    12→_MARKER_GLOB_CHARS = ("*", "?", "[")
    13→
    14→
    15→def make_lang_config(name: str, cfg_cls: type) -> LangConfig:
    16→    """Instantiate and validate a language config."""
    17→    try:
    18→        cfg = cfg_cls()
    19→    except (TypeError, ValueError, AttributeError, RuntimeError, OSError) as ex:
    20→        raise ValueError(
    21→            f"Failed to instantiate language config '{name}': {ex}"
    22→        ) from ex
    23→    validate_lang_contract(name, cfg)
    24→    return cfg
    25→
    26→
    27→def get_lang(name: str) -> LangConfig:
    28→    """Get a language config by name.
    29→
    30→    All plugins (full and generic) store LangConfig instances in the registry.
    31→    Test doubles that store plain classes are instantiated on demand as a fallback.
    32→    """
    33→    if not registry_state.is_registered(name):
    34→        load_all()
    35→    if not registry_state.is_registered(name):
    36→        available = ", ".join(sorted(registry_state.all_keys()))
    37→        raise ValueError(f"Unknown language: {name!r}. Available: {available}")
    38→    obj = registry_state.get(name)
    39→    if isinstance(obj, LangConfig):
    40→        return obj
    41→    return make_lang_config(name, obj)  # fallback for test doubles
    42→
    43→
    44→def auto_detect_lang(project_root: Path) -> str | None:
    45→    """Auto-detect language from project files.
    46→
    47→    When multiple config files are present (e.g. package.json + pyproject.toml),
    48→    counts actual source files to pick the dominant language instead of relying
    49→    on first-match ordering.
    50→    """
    51→    load_all()
    52→    candidates: list[str] = []
    53→    configs: dict[str, LangConfig] = {}
    54→
    55→    for lang_name, obj in registry_state.all_items():
    56→        cfg = obj if isinstance(obj, LangConfig) else make_lang_config(lang_name, obj)
    57→        configs[lang_name] = cfg
    58→        markers = getattr(cfg, "detect_markers", []) or []
    59→        if markers and any(_detect_marker_exists(project_root, marker) for marker in markers):
    60→            candidates.append(lang_name)
    61→
    62→    if not candidates:
    63→        # Marker-less fallback: pick language with most source files.
    64→        best, best_count = None, 0
    65→        for lang_name, cfg in configs.items():
    66→            count = len(cfg.file_finder(project_root))
    67→            if count > best_count:
    68→                best, best_count = lang_name, count
    69→        return best if best_count > 0 else None
    70→
    71→    if len(candidates) == 1:
    72→        return candidates[0]
    73→
    74→    # Multiple candidates: choose language with most source files.
    75→    best, best_count = None, -1
    76→    for lang_name in candidates:
    77→        count = len(configs[lang_name].file_finder(project_root))
    78→        if count > best_count:
    79→            best, best_count = lang_name, count
    80→    return best
    81→
    82→
    83→def _detect_marker_exists(project_root: Path, marker: str) -> bool:
    84→    marker_text = str(marker).strip()
    85→    if not marker_text:
    86→        return False
    87→
    88→    # Fast path for literal markers.
    89→    if (project_root / marker_text).exists():
    90→        return True
    91→
    92→    # Wildcard markers (for example "*.fsproj") are matched at project root.
    93→    if any(ch in marker_text for ch in _MARKER_GLOB_CHARS):
    94→        return any(project_root.glob(marker_text))
    95→    return False
    96→
    97→
    98→def available_langs() -> list[str]:
    99→    """Return list of registered language names."""
   100→    load_all()
   101→    return sorted(registry_state.all_keys())
   102→
```

1→"""Language plugin discovery and import error surfacing."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import importlib
     6→import importlib.util
     7→import logging
     8→from pathlib import Path
     9→
    10→from . import registry_state
    11→
    12→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    13→
    14→# Broader than PARSE_INIT_ERRORS: plugin imports may also raise SyntaxError/TypeError.
    15→_PLUGIN_IMPORT_ERRORS: tuple[type[Exception], ...] = (
    16→    ImportError, SyntaxError, ValueError, TypeError, RuntimeError, OSError,
    17→)
    18→
    19→
    20→def raise_load_errors() -> None:
    21→    errors = registry_state.get_load_errors()
    22→    if not errors:
    23→        return
    24→    lines = ["Language plugin import failures:"]
    25→    for module_name, ex in sorted(errors.items()):
    26→        lines.append(f"  - {module_name}: {type(ex).__name__}: {ex}")
    27→    raise ImportError("\n".join(lines))
    28→
    29→
    30→def load_all() -> None:
    31→    """Import all language modules to trigger registration."""
    32→    if registry_state.was_load_attempted():
    33→        raise_load_errors()
    34→        return
    35→
    36→    lang_dir = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
    37→    if lang_dir.name == "_framework":
    38→        lang_dir = lang_dir.parent
    39→    base_package = __package__.rsplit(".", 1)[0]
    40→    failures: dict[str, BaseException] = {}
    41→
    42→    # Discover single-file plugins by naming convention (e.g. plugin_rust.py).
    43→    for f in sorted(lang_dir.glob("plugin_*.py")):
    44→        module_name = f".{f.stem}"
    45→        try:
    46→            importlib.import_module(module_name, base_package)
    47→        except _PLUGIN_IMPORT_ERRORS as ex:
    48→            logger.debug("Language plugin import failed for %s: %s", module_name, ex)
    49→            failures[module_name] = ex
    50→
    51→    # Discover packages (e.g. lang/typescript/)
    52→    for d in sorted(lang_dir.iterdir()):
    53→        if (
    54→            d.is_dir()
    55→            and (d / "__init__.py").exists()
    56→            and not d.name.startswith("_")
    57→        ):
    58→            module_name = f".{d.name}"
    59→            try:
    60→                importlib.import_module(module_name, base_package)
    61→            except _PLUGIN_IMPORT_ERRORS as ex:
    62→                logger.debug(
    63→                    "Language package import failed for %s: %s", module_name, ex
    64→                )
    65→                failures[module_name] = ex
    66→
    67→    # Discover user plugins from <active-project-root>/.desloppify/plugins/*.py
    68→    try:
    69→        from desloppify.core._internal.text_utils import get_project_root
    70→
    71→        user_plugin_dir = get_project_root() / ".desloppify" / "plugins"
    72→        if user_plugin_dir.is_dir():
    73→            for f in sorted(user_plugin_dir.glob("*.py")):
    74→                spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location(
    75→                    f"desloppify_user_plugin_{f.stem}", f
    76→                )
    77→                if spec and spec.loader:
    78→                    try:
    79→                        mod = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec)
    80→                        spec.loader.exec_module(mod)
    81→                    except _PLUGIN_IMPORT_ERRORS as ex:
    82→                        logger.debug(
    83→                            "User plugin import failed for %s: %s", f.name, ex
    84→                        )
    85→                        failures[f"user:{f.name}"] = ex
    86→    except (OSError, ImportError) as exc:
    87→        logger.debug("User plugin discovery skipped: %s", exc)
    88→
    89→    registry_state.set_load_attempted(True)
    90→    registry_state.set_load_errors(failures)
    91→    raise_load_errors()
    92→
```

```
title:	review runner: codex exit 0 can produce missing_output with no result file
state:	OPEN
author:	peteromallet
labels:	
comments:	0
assignees:	
projects:	
milestone:	
number:	144
--
## Summary
`desloppify review --run-batches --runner codex` can mark batches as `missing_output` even when the codex process exits `0`.

This leaves subjective review stale because successful-looking batch executions produce no importable payload files.

## Repro command
Run in a repo with prepared review packet:

```bash
desloppify review --run-batches --runner codex --parallel --dimensions cross_module_architecture
```

## Actual behavior
- `run_summary.json` reports batch status `missing_output` with `exit_code: 0`
- No files are written under run `results/`
- Run aborts with failed batches and no import

Example from local run:
- `run_summary`: `.desloppify/subagents/runs/20260226_142225/run_summary.json`
- `run log`: `.desloppify/subagents/runs/20260226_142225/run.log`
- batch logs:
  - `.desloppify/subagents/runs/20260226_142225/logs/batch-1.log`
  - `.desloppify/subagents/runs/20260226_142225/logs/batch-2.log`

In that run, both batches had `exit_code: 0` and `status: missing_output`.

## Expected behavior
If codex exits `0`, `desloppify` should either:
1. Have a valid output file in `results/`, or
2. Recover payload from stdout/stderr/log and persist to `results/`, or
3. Treat it as retryable runner failure (not silent success) and retry according to retry settings.

## Why this matters
This can stall subjective scoring loops, since review batches appear to execute but produce no importable outputs, keeping strict score stale.

## Environment
- desloppify: local build from `peteromallet/desloppify` (current around 2026-02-26)
- codex CLI: `0.105.0`
- platform: macOS
```

858→def execute_batches(
   859→    *,
   860→    tasks: dict[int, object],
   861→    run_parallel: bool,
   862→    progress_fn=None,
   863→    error_log_fn=None,
   864→    max_parallel_workers: int | None = None,
   865→    heartbeat_seconds: float | None = 15.0,
   866→    clock_fn=time.monotonic,
   867→) -> list[int]:
   868→    """Run indexed tasks and return failed index list.
   869→
   870→    Each value in *tasks* is a zero-arg callable returning an int exit code.
   871→    All domain knowledge (files, prompts, etc.) is pre-bound by the caller.
   872→    """
   873→    indexes = sorted(tasks)
   874→    if run_parallel:
   875→        return _execute_parallel(
   876→            tasks=tasks,
   877→            indexes=indexes,
   878→            progress_fn=progress_fn,
   879→            error_log_fn=error_log_fn,
   880→            max_parallel_workers=max_parallel_workers,
   881→            heartbeat_seconds=heartbeat_seconds,
   882→            clock_fn=clock_fn,
   883→        )
   884→    return _execute_serial(
   885→        tasks=tasks,
   886→        indexes=indexes,
   887→        progress_fn=progress_fn,
   888→        error_log_fn=error_log_fn,
   889→        clock_fn=clock_fn,
   890→    )
   891→
   892→
   893→def _execute_serial(*, tasks, indexes, progress_fn, error_log_fn, clock_fn) -> list[int]:
   894→    """Run tasks one at a time — no threads, no closures."""
   895→    failures: set[int] = set()
   896→    for idx in indexes:
   897→        t0 = float(clock_fn())
   898→        start_error = _emit_progress(
   899→            progress_fn, idx, "start", None, details={"max_workers": 1}
   900→        )
   901→        if start_error is not None:
   902→            _record_execution_error(
   903→                error_log_fn=error_log_fn,
   904→                failures=failures,
   905→                idx=idx,
   906→                exc=start_error,
   907→            )
   908→        try:
   909→            code = tasks[idx]()
   910→        except Exception as exc:
   911→            _record_execution_error(
   912→                error_log_fn=error_log_fn,
   913→                failures=failures,
   914→                idx=idx,
   915→                exc=exc,
   916→            )
   917→            code = 1
   918→        if code != 0:
   919→            failures.add(idx)
   920→        done_error = _emit_progress(
   921→            progress_fn, idx, "done", code,
   922→            details={"elapsed_seconds": int(max(0.0, clock_fn() - t0))},
   923→        )
   924→        if done_error is not None:
   925→            _record_execution_error(
   926→                error_log_fn=error_log_fn,
   927→                failures=failures,
   928→                idx=idx,
   929→                exc=done_error,
   930→            )
   931→    return sorted(failures)
   932→
   933→
   934→def _execute_parallel(
   935→    *, tasks, indexes, progress_fn, error_log_fn,
   936→    max_parallel_workers, heartbeat_seconds, clock_fn,
   937→) -> list[int]:
   938→    """Run tasks in a thread pool with optional heartbeat monitoring.
   939→
   940→    Two closures (_run_one, _on_complete) share mutable executor state
   941→    (started_at, failures) under a lock — the natural pattern for thread-pool
   942→    work.
   943→    """
   944→    requested = (
   945→        int(max_parallel_workers)
   946→        if isinstance(max_parallel_workers, int) and max_parallel_workers > 0
   947→        else 8
   948→    )
   949→    max_workers = max(1, min(len(indexes), requested))
   950→    heartbeat = (
   951→        float(heartbeat_seconds)
   952→        if isinstance(heartbeat_seconds, int | float) and heartbeat_seconds > 0
   953→        else None
   954→    )
   955→
   956→    failures: set[int] = set()
   957→    progress_failures: set[int] = set()
   958→    started_at: dict[int, float] = {}
   959→    lock = threading.Lock()
   960→
   961→    def _on_progress_error(idx: int, err: Exception) -> None:
   962→        with lock:
   963→            progress_failures.add(idx)
   964→        if callable(error_log_fn):
   965→            try:
   966→                error_log_fn(idx, err)
   967→            except Exception:
   968→                pass
   969→
   970→    def _run_one(idx: int) -> int:
   971→        with lock:
   972→            started_at[idx] = float(clock_fn())
   973→        progress_error = _emit_progress(
   974→            progress_fn, idx, "start", None,
   975→            details={"max_workers": max_workers},
   976→        )
   977→        if progress_error is not None:
   978→            _on_progress_error(idx, progress_error)
   979→        return tasks[idx]()
   980→
   981→    def _on_complete(future) -> None:
   982→        idx = futures[future]
   983→        with lock:
   984→            t0 = started_at.get(idx, float(clock_fn()))
   985→        elapsed = int(max(0.0, clock_fn() - t0))
   986→        try:
   987→            code = future.result()
   988→        except Exception as exc:
   989→            _record_execution_error(
   990→                error_log_fn=error_log_fn,
   991→                failures=failures,
   992→                idx=idx,
   993→                exc=exc,
   994→            )
   995→            done_error = _emit_progress(
   996→                progress_fn, idx, "done", 1, details={"elapsed_seconds": elapsed}
   997→            )
   998→            if done_error is not None:
   999→                _on_progress_error(idx, done_error)
  1000→            return
  1001→        done_error = _emit_progress(
  1002→            progress_fn, idx, "done", code, details={"elapsed_seconds": elapsed}
  1003→        )
  1004→        if done_error is not None:
  1005→            _on_progress_error(idx, done_error)
  1006→        with lock:
  1007→            had_progress_failure = idx in progress_failures
  1008→        if code != 0 or had_progress_failure:
  1009→            failures.add(idx)

  1011→    with ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=max_workers) as executor:
  1012→        futures: dict = {}
  1013→        for idx in indexes:
  1014→            queue_error = _emit_progress(
  1015→                progress_fn, idx, "queued", None,
  1016→                details={"max_workers": max_workers},
  1017→            )
  1018→            if queue_error is not None:
  1019→                _on_progress_error(idx, queue_error)
  1020→                failures.add(idx)
  1021→            futures[executor.submit(_run_one, idx)] = idx
  1022→
  1023→        pending = set(futures.keys())
  1024→
  1025→        if heartbeat is None:
  1026→            for future in as_completed(pending):

  1029→

  1032→                future = next(as_completed(pending, timeout=heartbeat))

  1036→                    futures,
  1037→                    started_at,
  1038→                    lock,
  1039→                    indexes,
  1040→                    progress_fn,
  1041→                    clock_fn,
  1042→                    error_log_fn=error_log_fn,
  1043→                )
  1044→                continue
  1045→            pending.discard(future)
  1046→            _on_complete(future)
  1047→    return sorted(failures)
  1048→
  1049→
  1050→def _heartbeat(
  1051→    pending,
  1052→    futures,
  1053→    started_at,
  1054→    lock,
  1055→    indexes,
  1056→    progress_fn,
  1057→    clock_fn,

"""
Testing the bug scenario for extract_py_functions.
The issue is in the logic flow between _find_signature_end and extract_py_functions.
"""

# Scenario 1: Function signature without closing paren on any line
test_code_1 = """
def broken(
    param1,
    param2
    # No closing paren exists anywhere
"""

# Scenario 2: Function with colon but no closing paren
test_code_2 = """
def another_broken(
    param1,
    param2,
):  # <- This line has both ) and : so _find_signature_end returns here
# BUT if for some reason the join doesn't capture it...
"""

# Scenario 3: Function that ends with : but has no )
test_code_3 = """
def malformed(
    param1
    param2
):
"""

# Let's trace the actual bug:
lines_test = [
    "def broken(",
    "    param1,",
    "    param2",
    "    # No closing paren on this line either",
]

# _find_signature_end logic:
# Line 0: "def broken(" - has ( but no ) -> skip
# Line 1: "    param1," - no ) -> skip, j > start? Yes. endswith(":")?  No
# Line 2: "    param2" - no ) -> skip, j > start? Yes. endswith(":")?  No
# Line 3: "    # No..." - no ) -> skip, j > start? Yes. endswith(":")?  No
# Return None

# So the bug is actually caught at line 59-61 of extract_py_functions:
#    if j is None:
#        i += 1
#        continue

# BUT the issue title says "ValueError in extract_py_functions when signature has no closing paren"
# This means the function returns a non-None j when it shouldn't.

# Scenario 4: The actual bug scenario
lines_edge = [
    "def edge_case(",
    "    param1,",
    "    param2",
    "): pass  # <- This line has ) but is just ) followed by colon and pass",
]

# _find_signature_end logic on line 3:
# lt = "): pass"
# ")" in lt? Yes
# ":" in lt[lt.rindex(")") + 1:]? 
#   lt.rindex(")") = 0
#   lt[0+1:] = ": pass"
#   ":" in ": pass"? Yes
# Returns j=3

# Now in extract_py_functions:
# sig_text = lines[0:4] joined = "def edge_case(\n    param1,\n    param2\n): pass"
# open_paren = sig_text.index("(") = 11
# ")" in sig_text? Yes
# close_paren = sig_text.rindex(")") = ?
#   Find last ) in "def edge_case(\n    param1,\n    param2\n): pass"
#   Result: position of ) in "): pass"

# This should work... unless!

# Scenario 5: The REAL bug - when _find_signature_end returns a line with : but NO )
lines_real_bug = [
    "def incomplete(",
    "    param1,",
    "    param2",
    ":",  # <- This line has : and is > start, so line 28 returns j=3
]

# _find_signature_end on line 3:
# lt = ":"
# ")" in ":"? No
# j > start? Yes (3 > 0)
# lt.strip().endswith(":")?  Yes!
# Returns j=3

# Now in extract_py_functions:
# sig_text = "\n".join(lines[0:4]) = "def incomplete(\n    param1,\n    param2\n:"
# open_paren = sig_text.index("(") = 12
# ")" in sig_text? No  <- This check on line 66 catches it and continues
# So no crash...

# Scenario 6: Real crash - hybrid case
lines_crash = [
    "def messy(",
    "    param1,",
    "    param2,",  
    "    # comment without closing paren",
    ": ",  # Has colon but no paren
]

# Same as Scenario 5 - the check on line 66-68 protects against the crash.

# UNLESS... what if there's a ) in a comment AFTER the signature?
lines_maybe = [
    "def func(",
    "    param1",
    "):  # closing paren in comment? No wait...",
]

# Actually the issue might be about a different failure path.
# Let me re-read: "ValueError in extract_py_functions when signature has no closing paren"
# Line 66 says: if ")" not in sig_text: continue
# So there IS a guard!

# But wait - what if the guard works but close_paren = sig_text.rindex(")") on line 69
# still crashes AFTER the guard?

# That would only happen if:
# 1. Line 66: ")" in sig_text -> True (passes guard)
# 2. Line 69: sig_text.rindex(")") -> ValueError

# This can ONLY happen if ")" was removed from sig_text between lines 66 and 69!
# Or if there's a race condition (unlikely in single-threaded code).

# WAIT! I need to re-read the code. Let me check line 66 again.

print("The bug is subtle - let me trace through the actual flow...")
print()
print("The issue is in the logic of _find_signature_end line 27-28:")
print("    if j > start and lt.strip().endswith(':'):")
print("        return j")
print()
print("This returns a line index without checking if it contains a closing paren!")
print("Then in extract_py_functions:")
print("  - sig_text = join(lines[start:j+1])")
print("  - Line 66 checks: if ')' not in sig_text: continue")
print()
print("So the guard should catch it... UNLESS...")
print()
print("The real bug: _find_signature_end can return a line with ':' but without ')'")
print("And then sig_text might not have ')' if no prior lines had it either!")
print()
print("Example:")
print("  def incomplete(")
print("      x")
print("  :")
print()
print("Then sig_text = 'def incomplete(\\n    x\\n:'")
print("Has '('? Yes. Has ')'? No!")
print("Line 66 catches this: if ')' not in sig_text: continue")
print()
print("So the actual bug must be DIFFERENT...")
print()
print("Reading the issue title again: 'ValueError in extract_py_functions when sig_text.rindex(\")\")'")
print("Line 69: close_paren = sig_text.rindex(')')")
print()
print("WAIT! What if line 66 check is wrong?")
print("Line 66: if ')' not in sig_text:")
print("Line 69: close_paren = sig_text.rindex(')')")
print()
print("These should be safe together... unless there's a different code path!")
print()
print("AH! The fallback logic at line 27-28 in _find_signature_end:")
print("Returns j when line ENDS WITH ':' even if no ')' exists!")
print()
print("This is the bug: _find_signature_end can return without validating ')'")
print("Then extract_py_functions builds sig_text without ')'")
print("Then line 66 checks ')'... and PASSES because... no, it should fail")
print()
print("Unless line 66 is checking the WRONG variable!")
print()
print("FOUND IT!")
print("Line 66: if ')' not in sig_text: continue")
print("Line 69: close_paren = sig_text.rindex(')')")
print()
print("These are consistent! So no crash...")
print()
print("UNLESS the issue is about a DIFFERENT scenario:")
print("What if _find_signature_end line 25 crashes on rindex?")
print("Line 25: if ')' in lt and ':' in lt[lt.rindex(')') + 1:]:")
print()
print("YES! This line can crash if:")
print("  lt = ') # comment with ) in it : more'")
print("  ')' in lt -> True")
print("  ':' in lt[lt.rindex(')') + 1:] -> uses rindex to find LAST )")
print()
print("But that shouldn't crash either, unless... the issue is about")
print("line 25 trying to use rindex on a string that doesn't have ')'!")
print()
print("NO WAIT - line 25 has a guard: if ')' in lt")
print("So ')' exists. Then rindex won't crash.")
print()
print("The bug must be that line 27-28 fallback returns without ')' validation!")
```
