```
486→def run_codex_batch(
   487→    *,
   488→    prompt: str,
   489→    repo_root: Path,
   490→    output_file: Path,
   491→    log_file: Path,
   492→    deps: CodexBatchRunnerDeps,
   493→) -> int:
   494→    """Execute one codex batch and return a stable CLI-style status code."""
   495→    cmd = codex_batch_command(prompt=prompt, repo_root=repo_root, output_file=output_file)
   496→    retries_raw = deps.max_retries if isinstance(deps.max_retries, int) else 0
   497→    max_retries = max(0, retries_raw)
   498→    max_attempts = max_retries + 1
   499→    backoff_raw = (
   500→        float(deps.retry_backoff_seconds)
   501→        if isinstance(deps.retry_backoff_seconds, int | float)
   502→        else 0.0
   503→    )
   504→    retry_backoff_seconds = max(0.0, backoff_raw)
   505→    live_log_interval = (
   506→        float(deps.live_log_interval_seconds)
   507→        if isinstance(deps.live_log_interval_seconds, int | float)
   508→        and float(deps.live_log_interval_seconds) > 0
   509→        else 5.0
   510→    )
   511→    stall_seconds = (
   512→        int(deps.stall_after_output_seconds)
   513→        if isinstance(deps.stall_after_output_seconds, int | float)
   514→        and int(deps.stall_after_output_seconds) > 0
   515→        else 0
   516→    )
   517→    log_sections: list[str] = []
   518→    use_popen = bool(deps.use_popen_runner) and callable(
   519→        getattr(deps, "subprocess_popen", None)
   520→    )
   521→
   522→    for attempt in range(1, max_attempts + 1):
   523→        header = f"ATTEMPT {attempt}/{max_attempts}\n$ {' '.join(cmd)}"
   524→        started_monotonic = time.monotonic()
   525→        state = _RunnerState(last_stream_activity=started_monotonic)
   526→        ctx = _AttemptContext(

   528→            started_at_iso=datetime.now(UTC).isoformat(timespec="seconds"),
   529→            started_monotonic=started_monotonic,
   530→            output_file=output_file,
   531→            log_file=log_file,
   532→            log_sections=log_sections,
   533→            safe_write_text_fn=deps.safe_write_text_fn,

   535→        _write_live_snapshot(state, ctx)
   536→
   537→        if use_popen:
   538→            result = _run_via_popen(cmd, deps, state, ctx, live_log_interval, stall_seconds)
   539→        else:
   540→            result = _run_via_subprocess(cmd, deps, state, ctx, live_log_interval)

   609→def run_followup_scan(
   610→    *,
   611→    lang_name: str,
   612→    scan_path: str,
   613→    deps: FollowupScanDeps,
   614→) -> int:
   615→    """Run a follow-up scan and return a non-zero status when it fails."""
   616→    scan_cmd = [
   617→        deps.python_executable,
   618→        "-m",
   619→        "desloppify",
   620→        "--lang",
   621→        lang_name,
   622→        "scan",
   623→        "--path",
   624→        scan_path,
   625→    ]
   626→    print(deps.colorize_fn("\n  Running follow-up scan...", "bold"))
   627→    try:
   628→        result = deps.subprocess_run(
   629→            scan_cmd,
   630→            cwd=str(deps.project_root),
   631→            timeout=deps.timeout_seconds,
   632→        )
   633→    except deps.timeout_error:
   634→        print(
   635→            deps.colorize_fn(
   636→                f"  Follow-up scan timed out after {deps.timeout_seconds}s.",
   637→                "yellow",
   638→            ),
   639→            file=sys.stderr,
   640→        )
   641→        return 124
   642→    except OSError as exc:
   643→        print(
   644→            deps.colorize_fn(f"  Follow-up scan failed: {exc}", "red"),
   645→            file=sys.stderr,
   646→        )
   647→        return 1
   648→    return int(getattr(result, "returncode", 0) or 0)
   649→
   650→
   651→def write_packet_snapshot(
   652→    packet: dict,
   653→    *,
   654→    stamp: str,
   655→    review_packet_dir: Path,
   656→    blind_path: Path,
   657→    safe_write_text_fn,
   658→) -> tuple[Path, Path]:
   659→    """Persist immutable and blind packet snapshots for runner workflows."""
   660→    review_packet_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   661→    packet_path = review_packet_dir / f"holistic_packet_{stamp}.json"
   662→    safe_write_text_fn(packet_path, json.dumps(packet, indent=2) + "\n")
   663→    blind_packet = _build_blind_packet(packet)
   664→    safe_write_text_fn(blind_path, json.dumps(blind_packet, indent=2) + "\n")
   665→    return packet_path, blind_path
   666→
   667→
   668→def _build_blind_packet(packet: dict) -> dict:
   669→    """Return a blind-review packet with score anchoring metadata removed."""
   670→    blind = deepcopy(packet)
   671→    for key in _BLIND_PACKET_DROP_KEYS:
   672→        blind.pop(key, None)
   673→
   674→    config = blind.get("config")
   675→    if isinstance(config, dict):
   676→        sanitized = _sanitize_blind_config(config)
   677→        if sanitized:
   678→            blind["config"] = sanitized
   679→        else:
   680→            blind.pop("config", None)
   681→    return blind
   682→
   683→
   684→def build_blind_packet(packet: dict) -> dict:
   685→    """Public wrapper for blind packet sanitization."""
```

```
   255→def _write_lang_layout(
   256→    root: Path,
   257→    *,
   258→    missing_files: set[str] | None = None,
   259→    missing_dirs: set[str] | None = None,
   260→    missing_dir_inits: set[str] | None = None,
   261→    include_tests: bool = True,
   262→):
   263→    missing_files = missing_files or set()
   264→    missing_dirs = missing_dirs or set()
   265→    missing_dir_inits = missing_dir_inits or set()
   266→
   267→    for filename in lang_mod.REQUIRED_FILES:
   268→        if filename in missing_files:
   269→            continue
   270→        (root / filename).write_text("\n")
   271→
   272→    for dirname in lang_mod.REQUIRED_DIRS:
   273→        if dirname in missing_dirs:
   274→            continue
   275→        d = root / dirname
   276→        d.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   277→        if dirname not in missing_dir_inits:
   278→            (d / "__init__.py").write_text("\n")
   279→        if dirname == "tests" and include_tests:
   280→            (d / "test_smoke.py").write_text("def test_smoke():\n    assert True\n")
   281→

   385→def test_load_all_surfaces_import_failures(monkeypatch):
   386→    original_registry = dict(registry_state.all_items())
   387→    original_attempted = registry_state.was_load_attempted()
   388→    original_errors = registry_state.get_load_errors()
   389→    real_import_module = importlib.import_module

   393→            raise ImportError("simulated import failure")
   394→        return real_import_module(name, package)

   399→    registry_state.clear()
   400→
   401→    try:
   402→        with pytest.raises(ImportError, match="Language plugin import failures"):
   403→            load_all()
   404→    finally:
   405→        registry_state.clear()
```

> AGENT

Excellent! This test file (especially line 310-318) shows the exact problem and the test case for it. Now let me understand the issue better. The problem is:

1. The validation error is raised as a `ValueError` during `@register_lang` decorator application
2. This happens when the module is imported (during `load_all()`)

Excellent! Now I can see the issue clearly. Let me create a summary document with all the findings:

## Bug #144 Investigation Report

### Summary
The desloppify review runner has a bug where a codex process can exit with code 0 (success) but produce no result file, causing the batch to be marked as `missing_output` in `run_summary.json` even though the process succeeded.

### Relevant Files and Code Flow

#### 1. **Primary Bug Location: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/batches.py`**

**Lines 755-770** - Status determination logic in `do_run_batches()`:

```python
for idx in selected_indexes:
    key = str(idx + 1)
    state = batch_status.setdefault(
        key,
        {"position": batch_positions.get(idx, 0), "status": "pending"},
    )
    if idx not in failure_set:
        state["status"] = "succeeded"
        continue
    if idx in execution_failure_set:
        state["status"] = "failed"
        continue
    if not output_files[idx].exists():
        state["status"] = "missing_output"
        continue
    state["status"] = "parse_failed"
```

**The Problem:**
- A batch is marked "succeeded" ONLY if `idx not in failure_set` (line 761-763)
- `failure_set` is populated by `collect_batch_results_fn()` when parsing fails
- However, `execution_failure_set` is created from `execution_failures` returned by `execute_batches_fn()`
- The check at line 764-766 only sets status to "failed" if the batch is in `execution_failure_set`
- Lines 767-770 should never be reached because the conditions are:
  1. If not in failure_set → "succeeded" (continue)
  2. If in execution_failure_set → "failed" (continue)
  3. If file missing → "missing_output"

**But this is logically unreachable** because:
- If codex exits 0, the batch is NOT in execution_failure_set (lines 918-919 in runner_helpers.py)
- If the output file doesn't exist, the batch will be in failure_set from `collect_batch_results_fn()`
- So it should be marked "failed" by the collection function

#### 2. **Exit Code Handling: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/runner_helpers.py`**

**Lines 575-582** (in `run_codex_batch()`):
```python
if result.code == 0:
    deps.safe_write_text_fn(log_file, "\n\n".join(log_sections))
    return 0
```

**The Issue:**
- When codex exits with code 0, `run_codex_batch()` **immediately returns 0**
- It does NOT validate whether `output_file` actually exists
- It does NOT check whether the output file has valid JSON

**Lines 918-919** (in `_execute_serial()`):
```python
if code != 0:
    failures.add(idx)
```

**Lines 1008-1009** (in `_execute_parallel()`, `_on_complete()`):
```python
if code != 0 or had_progress_failure:
    failures.add(idx)
```

So if codex returns exit code 0, the batch index is NOT added to `execution_failures`.

#### 3. **Result Collection: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/runner_helpers.py`**

**Lines 1129-1181** (in `collect_batch_results()`):
```python
def collect_batch_results(
    *,
    selected_indexes: list[int],
    failures: list[int],
    output_files: dict[int, Path],
    allowed_dims: set[str],
    extract_payload_fn,
    normalize_result_fn,
) -> tuple[list[BatchResult], list[int]]:
    """Parse and normalize batch outputs, preserving prior failures."""
    batch_results: list[BatchResult] = []
    failure_set = set(failures)
    for idx in selected_indexes:
        had_execution_failure = idx in failure_set
        raw_path = output_files[idx]
        payload = None
        parsed_from_log = False
        if raw_path.exists():
            try:
                payload = extract_payload_fn(raw_path.read_text())
            except OSError:
                payload = None
        if payload is None:
            payload = _extract_payload_from_log(idx, raw_path, extract_payload_fn)
            parsed_from_log = payload is not None
        if payload is None:
            failure_set.add(idx)
            continue
        # ... rest of processing ...
```

When a batch with exit code 0 has no output file:
1. `raw_path.exists()` returns False
2. `payload = None`
3. `_extract_payload_from_log()` is called and likely returns None
4. `failure_set.add(idx)` is called (line 1155)

So the batch is correctly added to the failure set by the collection function.

#### 4. **Back to Batch Status Assignment: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/batches.py`**

**Lines 751-753**:
```python
execution_failure_set = set(execution_failures)
failure_set = set(failures)
successful_indexes = sorted(idx for idx in selected_indexes if idx not in failure_set)
```

Then **lines 755-770**:
- For batches NOT in failure_set: status = "succeeded"
- For batches in execution_failure_set: status = "failed"  
- For batches with missing output: status = "missing_output"
- Otherwise: status = "parse_failed"

### The Root Cause Logic Flow

When codex exits with code 0 but produces no output file:

1. **`execute_batches_fn()` returns**: empty list (code 0 means no execution failure)
2. **`collect_batch_results_fn()` returns**: batch index is added to failures due to no payload
3. **In `do_run_batches()` loop (lines 755-770)**:
   - `failure_set` contains the batch (from collection)
   - `execution_failure_set` is empty (exit code was 0)
   - The check `if idx not in failure_set` evaluates to False → don't set "succeeded"
   - The check `if idx in execution_failure_set` evaluates to False → don't set "failed"
   - The check `if not output_files[idx].exists()` evaluates to True → **set "missing_output"** ✓

**This is correct behavior!** But the issue is that the status should be "failed" or "execution_error", not "missing_output", since the process exited successfully but didn't produce a valid result.

### The Real Issue

The fundamental problem is **line 580-582** in `run_codex_batch()`:

```python
if result.code == 0:
    deps.safe_write_text_fn(log_file, "\n\n".join(log_sections))
    return 0
```

This returns success (0) **without validating that the output file exists and contains valid JSON**. 

When codex exits 0 but fails to write output, the process should:
1. Check if `_output_file_has_json_payload(output_file)` returned True
2. Return a non-zero status (e.g., 1 or a specific error code)
3. Or treat it as a recovery scenario like the timeout/stall cases (lines 550-574)

### Why This Matters

- Codex process can fail silently (crash, timeout internally, encounter API errors) but still exit 0
- desloppify interprets exit 0 as "batch ran successfully"
- The missing output file is only detected later during result parsing
- This gets marked as `missing_output` with `exit_code: 0` in the summary, which is misleading
- The batch should either be retried (if transient) or marked as a real failure (if persistent)

### Files to Review

1. `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/batches.py` (lines 755-770, 164-195)
2. `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/runner_helpers.py` (lines 486-607)
3. `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/runner_helpers.py` (lines 858-931, 934-1047)

```
"""Python extraction: function bodies, class structure, param patterns."""

import hashlib
import re
from pathlib import Path

from desloppify.engine.detectors.base import FunctionInfo
from desloppify.engine.detectors.passthrough import (
    classify_params,
    classify_passthrough_tier,
)
from desloppify.file_discovery import find_py_files
from desloppify.languages.python.extractors_classes import extract_py_classes
from desloppify.languages.python.extractors_shared import (
    extract_py_params,
    find_block_end,
    read_file,
)


def _find_signature_end(lines: list[str], start: int) -> int | None:
    """Find the line where a function signature closes."""
    for j in range(start, len(lines)):
        lt = lines[j]
        if ")" in lt and ":" in lt[lt.rindex(")") + 1 :]:
            return j
        if j > start and lt.strip().endswith(":"):
            return j
    return None


def _extract_py_return_annotation(sig_text: str) -> str | None:
    """Extract normalized return annotation from a Python signature."""
    m = re.search(r"\)\s*->\s*(.*?)\s*:", sig_text, re.DOTALL)
    if not m:
        return None
    annotation = " ".join(m.group(1).split())
    return annotation or None


def extract_py_functions(filepath: str) -> list[FunctionInfo]:
    """Extract function bodies from a Python file using indentation-based boundaries."""
    content = read_file(filepath)
    if content is None:
        return []
    lines = content.splitlines()
    functions = []
    fn_re = re.compile(r"^(\s*)(?:async\s+)?def\s+(\w+)\s*\(")
    i = 0
    while i < len(lines):
        m = fn_re.match(lines[i])
        if not m:
            i += 1
            continue
        fn_indent = len(m.group(1))
        name = m.group(2)
        start_line = i
        j = _find_signature_end(lines, i)
        if j is None:
            i += 1
            continue

        # Extract params from multi-line signature
        sig_text = "\n".join(lines[start_line : j + 1])
        open_paren = sig_text.index("(")
        close_paren = sig_text.rindex(")")
        param_str = sig_text[open_paren + 1 : close_paren]
        params = extract_py_params(param_str)

        # Find body extent: all lines indented past fn_indent, trim trailing blanks
        block_end = find_block_end(lines, j + 1, fn_indent)
        end_line = block_end
        while end_line > j + 1 and not lines[end_line - 1].strip():
            end_line -= 1
        body = "\n".join(lines[start_line:end_line])
        normalized = normalize_py_body(body)
        if len(normalized.splitlines()) >= 3:
            functions.append(
                FunctionInfo(
                    name=name,
                    file=filepath,
                    line=start_line + 1,
                    end_line=end_line,
                    loc=end_line - start_line,
                    body=body,
                    normalized=normalized,
                    body_hash=hashlib.md5(normalized.encode()).hexdigest(),
                    params=params,
                )
            )
        i = end_line
    return functions


def normalize_py_body(body: str) -> str:
    """Normalize a Python function body: strip docstrings, comments, print/logging."""
    lines = body.splitlines()
    normalized = []
    in_docstring = False
    docstring_quote = None
```

## Desloppify Bug #145 Investigation Report

### Issue Summary
The CLI hard-fails at startup when a language plugin is missing test files (specifically when the `tests/` directory doesn't contain at least one `test_*.py` file). This is a critical issue for pip installs from HEAD because any incomplete plugin breaks the entire tool.

### Root Cause Analysis

The problem occurs during **CLI startup in the plugin discovery phase**. Here's the exact failure chain:

**Call Stack:**
```
cli.py::main()
  → cli.py::create_parser()  [line 135]
    → cli.py::_create_parser(langs=available_langs()) [line 40]
      → languages/__init__.py::available_langs() [line 63]
        → languages/_framework/discovery.py::load_all() [line 30-91]
          → importlib.import_module('.gdscript', ...) [line 60]
            → gdscript/__init__.py::module execution [line 1-100]
              → @register_lang("gdscript") decorator [line 56]
                → languages/__init__.py::register_lang() [line 30-48]
                  → validate_lang_structure() [line 40]
                    → ValueError raised ✗ [line 29-32]
          → Caught as ImportError [line 61-65]
            → discovery.py::raise_load_errors() [line 20-27]
              → ImportError re-raised → CLI startup FAILS HARD
```

### Files Involved

#### 1. **Plugin Validation Code** (THE PROBLEM)
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/_framework/structure_validation.py`

**Lines 10-32:**
```python
def validate_lang_structure(lang_dir: Path, name: str) -> None:
    """Validate that a language plugin has all required files and directories."""
    errors: list[str] = []

    for filename in REQUIRED_FILES:
        if not (lang_dir / filename).is_file():
            errors.append(f"missing required file: {filename}")

    for dirname in REQUIRED_DIRS:
        target = lang_dir / dirname
        if not target.is_dir():
            errors.append(f"missing required directory: {dirname}/")
            continue
        if not (target / "__init__.py").is_file():
            errors.append(f"missing {dirname}/__init__.py")
        if dirname == "tests" and not any(target.glob("test_*.py")):  # ← LINE 25: THE CHECK
            errors.append("tests directory must contain at least one test_*.py file")  # ← LINE 26: ERROR MESSAGE

    if errors:
        raise ValueError(  # ← LINE 29: HARD FAILURE
            f"Language plugin '{name}' ({lang_dir.name}/) has structural issues:\n"
            + "\n".join(f"  - {e}" for e in errors)
        )
```

**Problem:** Line 25 checks if `tests/` directory contains any `test_*.py` files. Line 29 raises a `ValueError` that becomes a hard failure.

#### 2. **Registration Decorator** (CALLS VALIDATION)
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/__init__.py`

**Lines 30-48:**
```python
def register_lang(name: str) -> Callable[[T], T]:
    """Decorator to register a language config class.

    Validates structure, instantiates the class, validates the contract,
    and stores the *instance* in the registry.
    """

    def decorator(cls: T) -> T:
        module = inspect.getmodule(cls)
        if module and hasattr(module, "__file__"):
            validate_lang_structure(Path(module.__file__).parent, name)  # ← LINE 40: VALIDATION CALLED
        if isinstance(cls, type) and issubclass(cls, LangConfig):
            cfg = make_lang_config(name, cls)  # instantiate + validate
            registry_state.register(name, cfg)  # store instance
        else:
            registry_state.register(name, cls)  # test doubles
        return cls

    return decorator
```

**Problem:** Line 40 calls `validate_lang_structure()` during decorator execution (at module import time). If it raises `ValueError`, the entire import fails.

#### 3. **Plugin Discovery** (CATCHES BUT RE-RAISES)
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/_framework/discovery.py`

**Lines 14-27:**
```python
_PLUGIN_IMPORT_ERRORS: tuple[type[Exception], ...] = (
    ImportError, SyntaxError, ValueError, TypeError, RuntimeError, OSError,  # ← LINE 16: ValueError IS CAUGHT
)


def raise_load_errors() -> None:
    errors = registry_state.get_load_errors()
    if not errors:
        return
    lines = ["Language plugin import failures:"]
    for module_name, ex in sorted(errors.items()):
        lines.append(f"  - {module_name}: {type(ex).__name__}: {ex}")
    raise ImportError("\n".join(lines))  # ← LINE 27: RE-RAISES AS ImportError
```

**Lines 30-65:**
```python
def load_all() -> None:
    """Import all language modules to trigger registration."""
    if registry_state.was_load_attempted():
        raise_load_errors()  # ← RE-RAISES ERRORS
        return

    # ... discovery loop ...
    for d in sorted(lang_dir.iterdir()):
        if (
            d.is_dir()
            and (d / "__init__.py").exists()
            and not d.name.startswith("_")
        ):
            module_name = f".{d.name}"
            try:
                importlib.import_module(module_name, base_package)
            except _PLUGIN_IMPORT_ERRORS as ex:  # ← LINE 61: Catches ValueError
                logger.debug(
                    "Language package import failed for %s: %s", module_name, ex
                )
                failures[module_name] = ex  # ← Stores error

    # ... more code ...
    registry_state.set_load_attempted(True)
    registry_state.set_load_errors(failures)
    raise_load_errors()  # ← LINE 91: ALWAYS RAISES IF ANY ERRORS EXIST
```

**Problem:** The errors are stored in `failures` dict and later `raise_load_errors()` re-raises them as an `ImportError` (line 27). This is caught but **still prevents CLI startup** because it's raised in the `main()` function's call to `create_parser()` (line 135 in cli.py).

#### 4. **CLI Startup** (FAILS ON PARSER CREATION)
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/cli.py`

**Lines 123-150:**
```python
def main() -> None:
    # ... Unicode setup ...
    
    parser = create_parser()  # ← LINE 135: FAILS HERE if any plugin validation fails
    args = parser.parse_args()
    if args.command == "help":
        _handle_help_command(args, parser)
        return

    try:
        with runtime_scope():
            _resolve_default_path(args)
            _load_shared_runtime(args)

            handler = _resolve_handler(args.command)
            handler(args)
    except LangResolutionError as exc:
        print(colorize(f"  {exc.message}", "red"), file=sys.stderr)
        sys.exit(1)
    except KeyboardInterrupt:
        print("\nInterrupted.")
        sys.exit(1)
```

**Lines 38-40:**
```python
def create_parser():
    """Return the top-level argparse parser."""
    return _create_parser(langs=available_langs(), detector_names=_get_detector_names())
```

**Problem:** `available_langs()` calls `load_all()` which re-raises any plugin errors as `ImportError`. This exception is **NOT caught** and propagates up to crash the CLI.

#### 5. **Policy Definition** (ENFORCES THE REQUIREMENT)
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/_framework/policy.py`

**Lines 5-14:**
```python
REQUIRED_FILES: tuple[str, ...] = (
    "commands.py",
    "extractors.py",
    "phases.py",
    "move.py",
    "review.py",
    "test_coverage.py",
)

REQUIRED_DIRS: tuple[str, ...] = ("detectors", "fixers", "tests")  # ← "tests" is REQUIRED
```

**Problem:** "tests" is listed as a required directory, and `structure_validation.py` enforces that it must contain at least one `test_*.py` file. This is too strict for development scenarios.

### GDScript Plugin Current State

**Directory:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/gdscript/`

Current structure (VALID):
```
gdscript/
├── __init__.py
├── commands.py
├── extractors.py
├── phases.py
├── move.py
├── review.py
├── test_coverage.py
├── detectors/
│   ├── __init__.py
│   └── deps.py
├── fixers/
│   └── __init__.py
└── tests/
    ├── __init__.py
    └── test_init.py  ← ✓ Has test_*.py file (currently passes)
```

**Note:** The gdscript plugin currently PASSES validation because it has `tests/test_init.py`. The issue would occur if this file were deleted or never created initially.

### Why This Is a Problem

1. **pip install from HEAD fails:** If someone installs desloppify from development HEAD before a plugin's test file is committed, the entire CLI is unusable.
2. **No graceful degradation:** The tool completely fails instead of warning and continuing.
3. **Hard error during parser creation:** The error occurs during argument parsing, before any command logic can run.
4. **Not caught at CLI level:** The `main()` function in `cli.py` doesn't catch `ImportError` from plugin loading.

### What Needs to Change

Based on the current architecture, there are several potential fixes:

#### **Option 1: Convert to Warning (Most User-Friendly)**
- Change `validate_lang_structure()` to return warnings instead of raising `ValueError`
- Allow plugins to register with structural warnings
- Log warnings at startup but don't hard-fail
- **Locations to change:**
  - `desloppify/languages/_framework/structure_validation.py` - return list of warnings instead of raising
  - `desloppify/languages/__init__.py::register_lang()` - handle warning list
  - `desloppify/cli.py::main()` - catch/log ImportError for plugin loading

#### **Option 2: Make Test Files Optional**
- Remove the test file requirement from the policy
- **Locations to change:**
  - `desloppify/languages/_framework/structure_validation.py` lines 25-26 - remove tests/ check
  - `desloppify/languages/_framework/policy.py` line 14 - remove "tests" from REQUIRED_DIRS
  - Or make it a soft requirement

#### **Option 3: Wrap Plugin Discovery in Try/Except**
- Catch `ImportError` in `cli.py::create_parser()` and degrade gracefully
- **Locations to change:**
  - `desloppify/cli.py` lines 38-40 - wrap in try/except, log warnings
  - Or `desloppify/cli.py::main()` lines 135 - wrap parser creation

#### **Option 4: Defer Plugin Loading**
- Don't load all plugins at startup during parser creation
- Load plugins only when a specific language is needed
- **Locations to change:**
  - `desloppify/app/cli_support/parser.py` - change how available_langs is used
  - `desloppify/languages/_framework/discovery.py::load_all()` - defer to lazy loading

### Test Coverage for This Issue

**Test file:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/tests/lang/common/test_lang_init.py`

**Lines 310-318:**
```python
def test_validate_lang_structure_missing_tests_file(tmp_path):
    lang_dir = tmp_path / "dummy_lang"
    lang_dir.mkdir()
    _write_lang_layout(lang_dir, include_tests=False)

    with pytest.raises(
        ValueError, match=r"tests directory must contain at least one test_\*\.py file"
    ):
        lang_mod.validate_lang_structure(lang_dir, "dummy")
```

This test VERIFIES the hard-failure behavior exists, but there's no test for graceful handling.

### Summary

**Root cause:** The plugin validation in `structure_validation.py` raises a hard `ValueError` when tests directory lacks `test_*.py` files. This occurs during CLI startup in the plugin discovery phase and prevents the entire tool from running.

**Key files:**
1. `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/_framework/structure_validation.py` - Lines 25-26, 29-32 (the validation check)
2. `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/__init__.py` - Lines 40 (calls validation)
3. `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/_framework/discovery.py` - Lines 61-65, 91 (catches and re-raises)
4. `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/cli.py` - Lines 135, 38-40 (no error handling)
5. `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/_framework/policy.py` - Lines 14 (enforces tests as required)

**Recommended fix:** Make the validation produce warnings instead of hard failures, or defer plugin loading to command execution time rather than parser creation time.

## Report: Desloppify Bug #143 Investigation

### Executive Summary
Bug #143 was a `ValueError` crash in `extract_py_functions` when a function signature lacked a closing parenthesis. The bug has been **fixed** in the current codebase.

---

### The Bug

**Location:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/python/extractors.py`

**Affected Function:** `extract_py_functions` (lines 41-95)

**Root Cause:** 
The `_find_signature_end` function (lines 21-29) could return a line index without validating that the signature contained a closing parenthesis `)`. Specifically, the fallback logic at line 27-28:

```python
if j > start and lt.strip().endswith(":"):
    return j
```

This returns when a line ends with `:` (the end of a function definition) but **does not check** if a closing `)` exists anywhere in the collected signature.

**Scenario That Triggered The Bug:**

```python
def broken_sig(
    x,
    y
:  # Just a colon on its own line, no closing paren
    result = x + y
    return result
```

**Execution Flow:**
1. `_find_signature_end()` at line 25-26 checks: `if ")" in lt and ":" in lt[lt.rindex(")") + 1:]`
   - Line 3 (`":"`) has no `)`, so this check fails
2. Falls through to line 27-28: `if j > start and lt.strip().endswith(":")`
   - Line 3 satisfies this (j=3 > start=0, and `:` is the only non-whitespace)
   - **Returns j=3 without validating presence of `)`**

3. Back in `extract_py_functions`:
   - Line 64: `sig_text = "\n".join(lines[0:4])` → `"def broken_sig(\n    x,\n    y\n:"`
   - Line 65: `open_paren = sig_text.index("(")` → Works (found `(`)
   - Line 69 (OLD CODE): `close_paren = sig_text.rindex(")")` → **CRASHES!** ValueError: substring not found

**Old Code (Buggy - line 65-69 in prior version):**
```python
open_paren = sig_text.index("(")
close_paren = sig_text.rindex(")")  # ← CRASHES if ")" not in sig_text!
param_str = sig_text[open_paren + 1 : close_paren]
```

---

### The Fix

**Location:** Lines 66-68 (current version)

**Fixed Code:**
```python
open_paren = sig_text.index("(")
if ")" not in sig_text:           # ← GUARD ADDED
    i += 1
    continue
close_paren = sig_text.rindex(")")  # ← Now safe to call
```

**Why This Works:**
- The guard checks for `)` presence **before** calling `rindex()`
- If no closing paren exists, the function is skipped (returned as `None` - not extracted)
- This prevents the `ValueError: substring not found` crash

---

### Root Issue in `_find_signature_end`

The deeper architectural issue is that `_find_signature_end` has two different success criteria (lines 25 and 27):

1. **Line 25:** Requires both `)` AND `:` after it
2. **Line 27-28:** Only requires `:` at end of line (fallback)

The fallback is **too permissive** and doesn't validate the signature completeness. However, this is now mitigated by the guard in `extract_py_functions`.

---

### Current Test Coverage

The test file `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/python/tests/test_py_extractors.py` includes:
- Line 141-160: `test_multi_line_signature()` - tests normal multi-line signatures with proper closing parens
- **Missing:** No test for malformed signatures without closing parens

**Recommended Test Addition:**
```python
def test_multi_line_signature_missing_closing_paren(self, tmp_path):
    """Malformed signatures without closing paren should be skipped."""
    fp = _write_py(
        tmp_path,
        """\
        def broken_sig(
            param_a,
            param_b
        :
            x = param_a
            return x
    """,
    )
    funcs = extract_py_functions(fp)
    # Should skip the malformed function
    names = {f.name for f in funcs}
    assert "broken_sig" not in names
```

---

### Summary Table

| Aspect | Details |
|--------|---------|
| **Bug ID** | #143 |
| **Status** | ✅ FIXED |
| **Error Type** | `ValueError: substring not found` |
| **Crash Location** | Line 69 (`sig_text.rindex(")")`) |
| **Root Cause** | Missing guard before `rindex()` call |
| **Fix Applied** | Lines 66-68 guard check |
| **Fix Type** | Defensive programming (check before `.rindex()`) |
| **Impact** | Graceful skip of malformed signatures instead of crash |

```
1→"""Detect architectural layer violations via import-linter.
     2→
     3→Reads `.importlinter` config from the scanned project's root (walking up
     4→from the scan path to find the first `.importlinter` file, stopping at `.git`
     5→boundaries). Falls back gracefully to None when lint-imports is not installed
     6→or no config is found.
     7→
     8→See https://import-linter.readthedocs.io for config format.
     9→"""
    10→
    11→from __future__ import annotations
    12→
    13→import logging
    14→import re
    15→import subprocess
    16→from pathlib import Path
    17→
    18→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    19→
    20→_BROKEN_CONTRACT_RE = re.compile(r"Broken contract '([^']+)'")
    21→# import-linter outputs violations as "  module.a imports module.b"
    22→_VIOLATION_IMPORTS_RE = re.compile(r"^\s+(\S+)\s+imports\s+(\S+)\s*$")
    23→# Also handle "  module.a -> module.b" format (arrow notation)
    24→_VIOLATION_ARROW_RE = re.compile(r"^\s+(\S+)\s+->\s+(\S+)\s*$")
    25→
    26→
    27→def _find_importlinter_root(path: Path) -> Path | None:
    28→    """Walk up from path to find the directory containing .importlinter.
    29→
    30→    Stops when a .git boundary or the filesystem root is encountered.
    31→    """
    32→    candidate = path.resolve()
    33→    for directory in [candidate, *candidate.parents]:
    34→        if (directory / ".importlinter").exists():
    35→            return directory
    36→        # Stop at project root boundaries
    37→        if (directory / ".git").exists() or directory.parent == directory:
    38→            break
    39→    return None
    40→
    41→
    42→def _module_to_file(module: str) -> str:
    43→    """Convert dotted module name to a relative file path."""
    44→    return module.replace(".", "/") + ".py"
    45→
    46→
    47→def detect_with_import_linter(path: Path) -> list[dict] | None:
    48→    """Run lint-imports and return layer violation entries, or None on failure."""
    49→    config_dir = _find_importlinter_root(path)
    50→    if config_dir is None:
    51→        logger.debug("import-linter: no .importlinter config found — skipping")
    52→        return None
    53→
    54→    try:
    55→        result = subprocess.run(
    56→            ["lint-imports"],
    57→            capture_output=True,
    58→            text=True,
    59→            cwd=config_dir,
    60→            timeout=60,
    61→        )
    62→    except FileNotFoundError:
    63→        logger.debug("import-linter: lint-imports not found — skipping")
    64→        return None
    65→    except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
    66→        logger.debug("import-linter: timed out")
    67→        return None
    68→
    69→    if result.returncode == 0:
    70→        return []
    71→
    72→    output = result.stdout + result.stderr
    73→    if not output.strip():
    74→        return []
    75→
    76→    entries: list[dict] = []
    77→    current_contract: str | None = None
    78→
    79→    for line in output.splitlines():
    80→        m_contract = _BROKEN_CONTRACT_RE.search(line)
    81→        if m_contract:
    82→            current_contract = m_contract.group(1)
    83→            continue
    84→
    85→        if current_contract is None:
    86→            continue
    87→
    88→        m_viol = _VIOLATION_IMPORTS_RE.match(line) or _VIOLATION_ARROW_RE.match(line)
    89→        if not m_viol:
    90→            continue
    91→
    92→        source_mod = m_viol.group(1)
    93→        target_mod = m_viol.group(2)
    94→        source_file = _module_to_file(source_mod)
    95→        source_pkg = source_mod.rsplit(".", 1)[-1] if "." in source_mod else source_mod
    96→
    97→        entries.append(
    98→            {
    99→                "file": source_file,
   100→                "line": 0,
   101→                "summary": (
   102→                    f"Layer violation: {source_mod} -> {target_mod}"
   103→                    f" (contract: {current_contract})"
   104→                ),
   105→                "confidence": "high",
   106→                "source_pkg": source_pkg,
   107→                "target_pkg": target_mod,
   108→            }
   109→        )
   110→
   111→    return entries
   112→
   113→
   114→__all__ = ["detect_with_import_linter"]
   115→
```

```
    32→def _append_run_log(run_log_path: Path, message: str) -> None:
    33→    """Append a timestamped message to the run log file."""
    34→    line = f"{datetime.now(UTC).isoformat(timespec='seconds')} {message}\n"
    35→    try:
    36→        with run_log_path.open("a", encoding="utf-8") as handle:
    37→            handle.write(line)
    38→    except OSError as exc:
    39→        log_best_effort_failure(logger, f"append review run log {run_log_path}", exc)
    40→        if run_log_path not in _RUN_LOG_WARNED_PATHS:
    41→            _RUN_LOG_WARNED_PATHS.add(run_log_path)
    42→            warn_best_effort(
    43→                f"Could not append review run log at {run_log_path}: {exc}"
    44→            )
    45→        return
    46→
    47→
    48→@dataclass
    49→class _ProgressCtx:
    50→    """Shared mutable state for the batch progress reporter."""
    51→
    52→    run_log_path: Path
    53→    colorize_fn: Callable[[str, str], str]
    54→    total_batches: int
    55→    batch_positions: dict[int, int]
    56→    batch_status: dict[str, dict[str, object]]
    57→    stall_warning_seconds: int
    58→    stall_warned_batches: set[int]
    59→
    60→
    61→def _report_progress(
    62→    ctx: _ProgressCtx,
    63→    batch_index: int | object,
    64→    event: str | None = None,
    65→    code: int | None = None,
    66→    **details,
    67→) -> None:
    68→    """Handle batch progress events (heartbeat, queued, start, done)."""
    69→    if event is None and hasattr(batch_index, "event"):
    70→        progress_event = batch_index
    71→        event = str(getattr(progress_event, "event", "")).strip()
    72→        code = getattr(progress_event, "code", code)
    73→        details_obj = getattr(progress_event, "details", {})
    74→        details = details_obj if isinstance(details_obj, dict) else {}
    75→        batch_index = int(getattr(progress_event, "batch_index", -1))
    76→    else:
    77→        batch_index = int(batch_index) if isinstance(batch_index, int | float) else -1
    78→
    79→    if not event:
    80→        return
    81→
    82→    if event == "heartbeat":
    83→        _handle_heartbeat_event(ctx, details)
    84→        return
    85→
    86→    position = ctx.batch_positions.get(batch_index, 0)
    87→    key = str(batch_index + 1)
    88→    state = ctx.batch_status.setdefault(
    89→        key, {"position": position, "status": "pending"},
    90→    )
    91→    if event == "queued":
    92→        state["status"] = "queued"
    93→        print(ctx.colorize_fn(f"  Batch {position}/{ctx.total_batches} queued (#{batch_index + 1})", "dim"))
    94→        _append_run_log(ctx.run_log_path, f"batch-queued batch={batch_index + 1} position={position}/{ctx.total_batches}")
    95→        return
    96→    if event == "start":
    97→        state["status"] = "running"
    98→        state["started_at"] = datetime.now(UTC).isoformat(timespec="seconds")
    99→        print(ctx.colorize_fn(f"  Batch {position}/{ctx.total_batches} started (#{batch_index + 1})", "dim"))
   100→        _append_run_log(ctx.run_log_path, f"batch-start batch={batch_index + 1} position={position}/{ctx.total_batches}")
   101→        return
   102→    if event == "done":
   103→        _handle_done_event(ctx, batch_index, position, state, code, details)
   104→
   105→
   106→def _handle_heartbeat_event(ctx: _ProgressCtx, details: dict) -> None:
   107→    """Process a heartbeat progress event."""
   108→    active = details.get("active_batches")
   109→    queued = details.get("queued_batches", [])
   110→    elapsed = details.get("elapsed_seconds", {})
   111→    if not isinstance(active, list):
   112→        active = []
   113→    if not isinstance(queued, list):
   114→        queued = []
   115→    if not active and not queued:
   116→        return
   117→    segments: list[str] = []
   118→    for idx in active[:6]:
   119→        secs = 0
   120→        if isinstance(elapsed, dict):
   121→            raw_secs = elapsed.get(idx, 0)
   122→            secs = int(raw_secs) if isinstance(raw_secs, int | float) else 0
   123→        segments.append(f"#{idx + 1}:{secs}s")
   124→    if len(active) > 6:
   125→        segments.append(f"+{len(active) - 6} more")
   126→    queued_segment = f", queued {len(queued)}" if queued else ""
   127→    print(
   128→        ctx.colorize_fn(
   129→            f"  Batch heartbeat: {len(active)}/{ctx.total_batches} active{queued_segment} "
   130→            f"({', '.join(segments) if segments else 'running batches pending'})",
   131→            "dim",
   132→        )
   133→    )
   134→    _append_run_log(
   135→        ctx.run_log_path,
   136→        "heartbeat "
   137→        f"active={[idx + 1 for idx in active]} queued={[idx + 1 for idx in queued]} "
   138→        f"elapsed={{{', '.join(f'{idx + 1}:{elapsed.get(idx, 0)}' for idx in active)}}}",
   139→    )
   140→    if ctx.stall_warning_seconds > 0 and isinstance(elapsed, dict):
   141→        slow_active = [
   142→            idx for idx in active
   143→            if isinstance(elapsed.get(idx), int | float)
   144→            and int(elapsed.get(idx) or 0) >= ctx.stall_warning_seconds
   145→        ]
   146→        newly_warned = [idx for idx in slow_active if idx not in ctx.stall_warned_batches]
   147→        if newly_warned:
   148→            ctx.stall_warned_batches.update(newly_warned)
   149→            print(
   150→                ctx.colorize_fn(
   151→                    f"  Stall warning: batches {[idx + 1 for idx in sorted(newly_warned)]} exceeded "
   152→                    f"{ctx.stall_warning_seconds}s elapsed. "
   153→                    "This may be normal for long runs; review run.log and batch logs.",
   154→                    "yellow",
   155→                )
   156→            )
   157→            _append_run_log(
   158→                ctx.run_log_path,
   159→                f"stall-warning threshold={ctx.stall_warning_seconds}s "
   160→                f"batches={[idx + 1 for idx in sorted(newly_warned)]}",
   161→            )
   162→
   163→

   185→    print(

   188→            tone,
   189→        )
   190→    )
   191→    _append_run_log(
   192→        ctx.run_log_path,
   193→        f"batch-done batch={batch_index + 1} position={position}/{ctx.total_batches} "
   194→        f"code={code} elapsed={state.get('elapsed_seconds', 0)}",
   195→    )
```

```
1→"""Python extraction: function bodies, class structure, param patterns."""
     2→
     3→import hashlib
     4→import re
     5→from pathlib import Path
     6→
     7→from desloppify.engine.detectors.base import FunctionInfo
     8→from desloppify.engine.detectors.passthrough import (
     9→    classify_params,
    10→    classify_passthrough_tier,
    11→)
    12→from desloppify.core.discovery_api import find_py_files
    13→from desloppify.languages.python.extractors_classes import extract_py_classes
    14→from desloppify.languages.python.extractors_shared import (
    15→    extract_py_params,
    16→    find_block_end,
    17→    read_file,
    18→)
    19→
    20→
    21→def _find_signature_end(lines: list[str], start: int) -> int | None:
    22→    """Find the line where a function signature closes."""
    23→    for j in range(start, len(lines)):
    24→        lt = lines[j]
    25→        if ")" in lt and ":" in lt[lt.rindex(")") + 1 :]:
    26→            return j
    27→        if j > start and lt.strip().endswith(":"):
    28→            return j
    29→    return None
    30→
    31→
    32→def _extract_py_return_annotation(sig_text: str) -> str | None:
    33→    """Extract normalized return annotation from a Python signature."""
    34→    m = re.search(r"\)\s*->\s*(.*?)\s*:", sig_text, re.DOTALL)
    35→    if not m:
    36→        return None
    37→    annotation = " ".join(m.group(1).split())
    38→    return annotation or None
    39→
    40→
    41→def extract_py_functions(filepath: str) -> list[FunctionInfo]:
    42→    """Extract function bodies from a Python file using indentation-based boundaries."""
    43→    content = read_file(filepath)
    44→    if content is None:
    45→        return []
    46→    lines = content.splitlines()
    47→    functions = []
    48→    fn_re = re.compile(r"^(\s*)(?:async\s+)?def\s+(\w+)\s*\(")
    49→    i = 0
    50→    while i < len(lines):
    51→        m = fn_re.match(lines[i])
    52→        if not m:
    53→            i += 1
    54→            continue
    55→        fn_indent = len(m.group(1))
    56→        name = m.group(2)
    57→        start_line = i
    58→        j = _find_signature_end(lines, i)
    59→        if j is None:
    60→            i += 1
    61→            continue
    62→
    63→        # Extract params from multi-line signature
    64→        sig_text = "\n".join(lines[start_line : j + 1])
    65→        open_paren = sig_text.index("(")
    66→        if ")" not in sig_text:
    67→            i += 1
    68→            continue
    69→        close_paren = sig_text.rindex(")")
    70→        param_str = sig_text[open_paren + 1 : close_paren]
    71→        params = extract_py_params(param_str)
    72→
    73→        # Find body extent: all lines indented past fn_indent, trim trailing blanks
    74→        block_end = find_block_end(lines, j + 1, fn_indent)
    75→        end_line = block_end
    76→        while end_line > j + 1 and not lines[end_line - 1].strip():
    77→            end_line -= 1
    78→        body = "\n".join(lines[start_line:end_line])
    79→        normalized = normalize_py_body(body)
    80→        if len(normalized.splitlines()) >= 3:
    81→            functions.append(
    82→                FunctionInfo(
    83→                    name=name,
    84→                    file=filepath,
    85→                    line=start_line + 1,
    86→                    end_line=end_line,
    87→                    loc=end_line - start_line,
    88→                    body=body,
    89→                    normalized=normalized,
    90→                    body_hash=hashlib.md5(normalized.encode()).hexdigest(),
    91→                    params=params,
    92→                )
    93→            )
    94→        i = end_line
    95→    return functions
    96→
    97→
    98→def normalize_py_body(body: str) -> str:
    99→    """Normalize a Python function body: strip docstrings, comments, print/logging."""
   100→    lines = body.splitlines()
```

```
    20→def raise_load_errors() -> None:
    21→    errors = registry_state.get_load_errors()
    22→    if not errors:
    23→        return
    24→    lines = ["Language plugin import failures:"]
    25→    for module_name, ex in sorted(errors.items()):
    26→        lines.append(f"  - {module_name}: {type(ex).__name__}: {ex}")
    27→    raise ImportError("\n".join(lines))

    29→
    30→def load_all() -> None:
    31→    """Import all language modules to trigger registration."""
    32→    if registry_state.was_load_attempted():
    33→        raise_load_errors()
    34→        return
    35→
    36→    lang_dir = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
    37→    if lang_dir.name == "_framework":
    38→        lang_dir = lang_dir.parent
    39→    base_package = __package__.rsplit(".", 1)[0]
    40→    failures: dict[str, BaseException] = {}
    41→
    42→    # Discover single-file plugins by naming convention (e.g. plugin_rust.py).
    43→    for f in sorted(lang_dir.glob("plugin_*.py")):
    44→        module_name = f".{f.stem}"
    45→        try:
    46→            importlib.import_module(module_name, base_package)
    47→        except _PLUGIN_IMPORT_ERRORS as ex:
    48→            logger.debug("Language plugin import failed for %s: %s", module_name, ex)
    49→            failures[module_name] = ex
    50→
    51→    # Discover packages (e.g. lang/typescript/)
    52→    for d in sorted(lang_dir.iterdir()):
    53→        if (
    54→            d.is_dir()
    55→            and (d / "__init__.py").exists()
    56→            and not d.name.startswith("_")
    57→        ):
    58→            module_name = f".{d.name}"
    59→            try:
    60→                importlib.import_module(module_name, base_package)
    61→            except _PLUGIN_IMPORT_ERRORS as ex:
    62→                logger.debug(
    63→                    "Language package import failed for %s: %s", module_name, ex
    64→                )
    65→                failures[module_name] = ex
    66→
    67→    # Discover user plugins from <active-project-root>/.desloppify/plugins/*.py
    68→    try:
    69→        from desloppify.core._internal.text_utils import get_project_root
    70→
    71→        user_plugin_dir = get_project_root() / ".desloppify" / "plugins"
    72→        if user_plugin_dir.is_dir():
    73→            for f in sorted(user_plugin_dir.glob("*.py")):
    74→                spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location(
    75→                    f"desloppify_user_plugin_{f.stem}", f
    76→                )
    77→                if spec and spec.loader:
    78→                    try:
    79→                        mod = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec)
    80→                        spec.loader.exec_module(mod)
    81→                    except _PLUGIN_IMPORT_ERRORS as ex:
    82→                        logger.debug(
    83→                            "User plugin import failed for %s: %s", f.name, ex
    84→                        )
    85→                        failures[f"user:{f.name}"] = ex
    86→    except (OSError, ImportError) as exc:
    87→        logger.debug("User plugin discovery skipped: %s", exc)
    88→
    89→    registry_state.set_load_attempted(True)
    90→    registry_state.set_load_errors(failures)
    91→    raise_load_errors()
    92→
```

```
    33→    """Extract normalized return annotation from a Python signature."""
    34→    m = re.search(r"\)\s*->\s*(.*?)\s*:", sig_text, re.DOTALL)
    35→    if not m:
```

```
   208→def _output_file_has_json_payload(output_file: Path) -> bool:
   209→    """Return True when the output file contains a valid JSON object."""
   210→    if not output_file.exists():
   211→        return False
   212→    try:
   213→        payload = json.loads(output_file.read_text())
   214→    except (OSError, json.JSONDecodeError):
   215→        return False
   216→    return isinstance(payload, dict)
   217→
   218→
   219→def _terminate_process(process: subprocess.Popen[str]) -> None:
   220→    """Terminate (then kill) a subprocess that may still be running."""
   221→    if process.poll() is not None:
   222→        return
   223→    try:
   224→        process.terminate()
   225→        process.wait(timeout=3)
   226→        return
   227→    except (OSError, subprocess.SubprocessError):
   228→        pass
   229→    try:
   230→        process.kill()
   231→        process.wait(timeout=3)
   232→    except (OSError, subprocess.SubprocessError):
   233→        return
   234→
   235→
   236→def _drain_stream(stream, sink: list[str], state: _RunnerState) -> None:
   237→    """Read lines from *stream* into *sink*, updating activity timestamp."""
   238→    if stream is None:
   239→        return
   240→    try:
   241→        for chunk in iter(stream.readline, ""):
   242→            if not chunk:
   243→                break
   244→            with state.lock:
   245→                sink.append(chunk)
   246→                state.last_stream_activity = time.monotonic()
   247→    except (OSError, ValueError) as exc:  # pragma: no cover - defensive boundary
   248→        with state.lock:
   249→            sink.append(f"\n[stream read error: {exc}]\n")
```

```
1→"""External command execution helpers for generic language plugins."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import logging
     6→import re
     7→import shlex
     8→import subprocess
     9→from collections.abc import Callable
    10→from dataclasses import dataclass
    11→from pathlib import Path
    12→from typing import Literal
    13→
    14→from desloppify.languages._framework.generic_parts.parsers import ToolParserError
    15→
    16→SubprocessRun = Callable[..., subprocess.CompletedProcess[str]]
    17→
    18→_SHELL_META_CHARS = re.compile(r"[|&;<>()$`\\n]")
    19→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    20→
    21→
    22→@dataclass(frozen=True)
    23→class ToolRunResult:
    24→    """Structured execution result for generic-tool detector commands."""
    25→
    26→    entries: list[dict]
    27→    status: Literal["ok", "empty", "error"]
    28→    error_kind: str | None = None
    29→    message: str | None = None
    30→    returncode: int | None = None
    31→
    32→
    33→def resolve_command_argv(cmd: str) -> list[str]:
    34→    """Return argv for subprocess.run without relying on shell=True."""
    35→    if _SHELL_META_CHARS.search(cmd):
    36→        return ["/bin/sh", "-lc", cmd]
    37→    try:
    38→        argv = shlex.split(cmd, posix=True)
    39→    except ValueError:
    40→        return ["/bin/sh", "-lc", cmd]
    41→    return argv if argv else ["/bin/sh", "-lc", cmd]
    42→
    43→
    44→def _output_preview(output: str, *, limit: int = 160) -> str:
    45→    """Return a compact one-line preview of tool output for diagnostics."""
    46→    text = " ".join(output.split())
    47→    if len(text) <= limit:
    48→        return text
    49→    return f"{text[:limit].rstrip()}..."
    50→
    51→
    52→def run_tool_result(
    53→    cmd: str,
    54→    path: Path,
    55→    parser: Callable[[str, Path], list[dict]],
    56→    *,
    57→    run_subprocess: SubprocessRun | None = None,
    58→) -> ToolRunResult:
    59→    """Run an external tool and parse its output with explicit failure status."""
    60→    runner = run_subprocess or subprocess.run
    61→    try:
    62→        result = runner(
    63→            resolve_command_argv(cmd),
    64→            shell=False,
    65→            cwd=str(path),
    66→            capture_output=True,
    67→            text=True,
    68→            timeout=120,
    69→        )
    70→    except FileNotFoundError as exc:
    71→        return ToolRunResult(
    72→            entries=[],
    73→            status="error",
    74→            error_kind="tool_not_found",
    75→            message=str(exc),
    76→        )
    77→    except subprocess.TimeoutExpired as exc:
    78→        return ToolRunResult(
    79→            entries=[],
    80→            status="error",
    81→            error_kind="tool_timeout",
    82→            message=str(exc),
    83→        )
    84→    output = (result.stdout or "") + (result.stderr or "")
    85→    if not output.strip():
    86→        if result.returncode not in (0, None):
    87→            return ToolRunResult(
    88→                entries=[],
    89→                status="error",
    90→                error_kind="tool_failed_no_output",
    91→                message=f"tool exited with code {result.returncode} and produced no output",
    92→                returncode=result.returncode,
    93→            )
    94→        return ToolRunResult(
    95→            entries=[],
    96→            status="empty",
    97→            returncode=result.returncode,
    98→        )
    99→    try:
   100→        parsed = parser(output, path)
   101→    except ToolParserError as exc:
   102→        logger.debug("Parser decode error for tool output: %s", exc)
   103→        return ToolRunResult(
   104→            entries=[],
   105→            status="error",
   106→            error_kind="parser_error",
   107→            message=str(exc),
   108→            returncode=result.returncode,
   109→        )
   110→    except (ValueError, TypeError, KeyError, AttributeError) as exc:
   111→        logger.debug("Skipping tool output due to parser exception: %s", exc)
   112→        return ToolRunResult(
   113→            entries=[],
   114→            status="error",
   115→            error_kind="parser_exception",
   116→            message=str(exc),
   117→            returncode=result.returncode,
   118→        )
   119→    if not isinstance(parsed, list):
   120→        return ToolRunResult(
   121→            entries=[],
   122→            status="error",
   123→            error_kind="parser_shape_error",
   124→            message="parser returned non-list output",
   125→            returncode=result.returncode,
   126→        )
   127→    if not parsed:
   128→        if result.returncode not in (0, None):
   129→            preview = _output_preview(output)
   130→            return ToolRunResult(
   131→                entries=[],
   132→                status="error",
   133→                error_kind="tool_failed_unparsed_output",
   134→                message=(
   135→                    f"tool exited with code {result.returncode} and produced no parseable entries"
   136→                    + (f": {preview}" if preview else "")
   137→                ),
   138→                returncode=result.returncode,
   139→            )
   140→        return ToolRunResult(
   141→            entries=[],
   142→            status="empty",
   143→            returncode=result.returncode,
   144→        )
   145→    return ToolRunResult(
   146→        entries=parsed,
   147→        status="ok",
   148→        returncode=result.returncode,
   149→    )
   150→
```

```
    75→def _is_excluded_path(path: Path) -> bool:
    76→    """True when path is under a known excluded directory."""
    77→    return any(part in _PROJECT_EXCLUSIONS for part in path.parts)
    78→
    79→
    80→def _find_csproj_files(path: Path) -> list[Path]:
    81→    """Find .csproj files under path, excluding build artifact directories."""
    82→    found: list[Path] = []
    83→    for p in path.rglob("*.csproj"):
    84→        if _is_excluded_path(p):
    85→            continue
    86→        found.append(p.resolve())
    87→    return sorted(found)

    90→def _parse_csproj_references(csproj_file: Path) -> tuple[set[Path], str | None]:
    91→    """Parse ProjectReference includes and optional RootNamespace."""
    92→    refs: set[Path] = set()
    93→    root_ns: str | None = None
    94→    try:
    95→        root = ET.parse(csproj_file).getroot()
    96→    except (ET.ParseError, OSError):
    97→        return refs, root_ns
    98→
    99→    for elem in root.iter():
   100→        tag = elem.tag.split("}", 1)[-1]
   101→        if tag == "ProjectReference":
   102→            include = elem.attrib.get("Include")
   103→            if include:
   104→                include_path = include.replace("\\", "/")
   105→                refs.add((csproj_file.parent / include_path).resolve())
   106→        elif tag == "RootNamespace":
   107→            if elem.text and elem.text.strip():
   108→                root_ns = elem.text.strip()
   109→    return refs, root_ns

   112→def _resolve_project_ref_path(raw_ref: str, base_dirs: tuple[Path, ...]) -> Path | None:
   113→    """Resolve a .csproj path against a list of base directories."""
   114→    ref = (raw_ref or "").strip().strip('"').replace("\\", "/")
   115→    if not ref:
   116→        return None
   117→    if not ref.lower().endswith(".csproj"):
   118→        return None
   119→    ref_path = Path(ref)
   120→    if ref_path.is_absolute():
   121→        try:
   122→            return ref_path.resolve()
   123→        except OSError as exc:
   124→            logger.debug(
   125→                "Skipping unresolved absolute project reference %s: %s", ref_path, exc
   126→            )
   127→            return None
   128→    fallback: Path | None = None
   129→    for base_dir in base_dirs:
   130→        try:
   131→            candidate = (base_dir / ref_path).resolve()
   132→        except OSError as exc:
   133→            logger.debug(
   134→                "Skipping unresolved project reference %s under %s: %s",
   135→                ref_path,
   136→                base_dir,
   137→                exc,
   138→            )
   139→            continue
   140→        if candidate.exists():
   141→            return candidate
   142→        if fallback is None:
   143→            fallback = candidate
   144→    return fallback
```

## Research Report: Exit Code Trusting, Index/Rindex Crashes, and Hard-Fail Patterns

Based on a comprehensive search of `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify`, I found the following patterns:

### 1. Exit Code 0 Trusted Without Output File Validation

**Critical Finding: `runner_helpers.py` line 580**
- **File**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/runner_helpers.py`
- **Line**: 580
- **Pattern**: `if result.code == 0:`
- **Context**: In the `_handle_run_result()` function, when a codex batch runner exits with code 0, the function immediately returns 0 without validating that the output file actually exists or contains valid JSON data.
- **Risk**: This is the exact pattern you warned about in issue #144. Exit code 0 is trusted completely, but the output file (`output_file` parameter) is never checked at this point. However, note that at lines 550 and 566, there ARE checks via `_output_file_has_json_payload(output_file)` for timeout and stall recovery scenarios.
- **Verdict**: **BUG CONFIRMED** — Unlike timeout/stall paths which validate `_output_file_has_json_payload()`, the normal exit-code-0 path at line 580-582 does no validation.

**Other Exit Code 0 Checks (Lower Risk)**:
- **File**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/python/detectors/import_linter_adapter.py`
  - **Line**: 69
  - **Pattern**: `if result.returncode == 0: return []`
  - **Context**: When `lint-imports` exits with code 0, returns empty list. This is safe because exit 0 means "no violations found" by design.
  - **Verdict**: **SAFE** — Semantically correct for this tool.

- **File**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/csharp/detectors/deps.py`
  - **Line**: 412
  - **Pattern**: `if proc.returncode != 0: return None`
  - **Context**: Roslyn payload parsing returns None on non-zero exit. Safe fallback behavior.
  - **Verdict**: **SAFE** — Defensive; doesn't trust output on failure.

- **File**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/_framework/generic_parts/tool_runner.py`
  - **Lines**: 86, 128
  - **Pattern**: `if result.returncode not in (0, None):`
  - **Context**: Only treats returncode 0 or None as success; all other codes trigger error status. Parsed output is validated on lines 119-126.
  - **Verdict**: **SAFE** — Properly decoupled from exit code.

### 2. Unchecked `.rindex()` and `.index()` Calls Outside Tests

Found **2 critical unchecked calls** in non-test production code:

**File**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/python/extractors.py`

1. **Line 25** - Unchecked `.rindex(")"`
   ```python
   if ")" in lt and ":" in lt[lt.rindex(")") + 1 :]:
   ```
   - **Risk**: The guard `")" in lt` checks for presence, BUT `.rindex()` is called AFTER this check. However, the substring check `lt[lt.rindex(")") + 1:]` doesn't check if that substring exists before slicing. If `")"` is the last character, the slice `lt[pos+1:]` returns empty string, which is safe. But if the logic expected a colon and there isn't one, silent logic failure occurs.
   - **Verdict**: **POTENTIAL BUG** — Safe from crash but may mask logic errors.

2. **Line 65** - Unchecked `.index("(")`
   ```python
   open_paren = sig_text.index("(")
   ```
   - **Precondition**: Called within `extract_py_functions()` after extracting multi-line signature text from lines 64. No guard check if `"("` exists.
   - **Risk**: **CRASH RISK** — If regex fails to match or input is malformed, `ValueError` on missing `"("`.
   - **Verdict**: **BUG CONFIRMED** — No try/except or guard.

3. **Line 69** - Unchecked `.rindex(")")`
   ```python
   close_paren = sig_text.rindex(")")
   ```
   - **Precondition**: Line 66 checks `if ")" not in sig_text: ... continue` which guards this.
   - **Verdict**: **SAFE** — Protected by line 66 guard.

### 3. Hard-Fail `raise ImportError` in Plugin/Discovery Code

**File**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/_framework/discovery.py`

- **Line 27**: `raise ImportError("\n".join(lines))`
  - **Context**: In `raise_load_errors()` function, explicitly raises `ImportError` when plugin import failures are detected.
  - **Scope**: Called in `load_all()` at line 91 (end of load sequence) and line 33 (early check).
  - **Verdict**: **INTENTIONAL HARD-FAIL** — By design. This is a "fail-fast" pattern for plugin initialization. The error is pre-collected via exception handling on lines 46-49 and 61-65, so individual plugin failures are caught, but if ANY failures exist, the entire system fails. This is appropriate for initialization, but prevents **graceful plugin degradation** (the subject of issue #145).

**Test-only instances** (not a concern):
- `desloppify/tests/core/test_hook_registry.py:43` — Mock raising `ImportError`
- `desloppify/tests/lang/common/test_lang_init.py:393` — Mock raising `ImportError`

## Recommendations

1. **runner_helpers.py:580** — Add validation: `if result.code == 0: if _output_file_has_json_payload(output_file): return 0 else: return 1`
2. **extractors.py:65** — Add guard: `if "(" not in sig_text: continue` before calling `.index("(")`
3. **discovery.py:27** — Replace hard-fail with logging + graceful fallback to allow partial plugin loading (issue #145)
